# Decision Log

## Purpose
The index of every decision in the Founder OS. Not optional (decision-rules §9): if a decision file exists, it is listed here; if it is listed here, the file exists. This is a default startup file (usage-rules §2).

Before creating a new `DEC-*` file, an operator must also fetch current
remote references and check `/07-decisions/decision-id-reservations.md`, per
`/01-constitution/decision-rules.md` §13 — a canonical identifier must be
unique across all active branches and workstreams, not just the commits
already present in this log.

## Owner
Founder. `builder` maintains entries; decision content enters via Founder-approved decisions (provenance: historically drafted via the Plato persona).

## Used By / Read By
All active roles — checked before any meaningful work.

## Written By
`builder` (log mechanics), per decision-rules; decision content via Founder-approved decisions.

## Update Cadence
Weekly review of health/drift (WF-10); updated immediately when decisions are created or change status.

## Current Status

**v4.97 (2026-10-25) — `DEC-20261020-01` implemented for the Grok models: `grok-4.6`, `grok-4.7` and `grok-4.7-build-fast` registered.** No new decision. `/04-agents/model-registry.md` `v0.24` → `v0.25` gains an entry for each, built from xAI's documentation read on 2026-10-25 (the grok-4.6 and grok-4.7 model pages, the Grok 4.7 guide and the pricing page), with an `independent-reviewer` Tier-2 binding on the `grok-build:xai` lane. That registration is what `DEC-20261020-01`'s clause 2 table requires ("on registration") before each model joins the lane's allowlist, following the Founder's answer of 2026-10-21 ("Add served models"). Each is public-class only while xAI's data-retention terms stay unverified (data-boundary-policy §5 item 4). xAI documents Grok 4.7 Fast but publishes no ID for it; the entry uses the id string `grok-build` reports, per the Founder's selection in session on 2026-10-25 (option label: "Own entry, ID from surface (Recommended)"). `claude-fable-5.5` gets no entry: Anthropic's models overview, read 2026-10-25, lists no Fable 5.5 (option label: "Leave it out (Recommended)"). The `independent-reviewer` role records (`/04-agents/role-registry.md` `v0.24`, `/04-agents/roles/independent-reviewer.md` `v0.8`), which still listed the `DEC-20260719-02` v1.1 Tier-2 roster as closed, now point to the registry's *Lanes* section as the roster and keep the v1.1 list as dated history; neither `DEC-20261019-01` nor `DEC-20261020-01` had updated them (Copilot review 5640800307; the Founder's selection of 2026-10-25, option label: "Point them to Lanes (Recommended)"). No roster change of its own. `DEC-20261020-01` `v0.15` updates its Next Action. Tier-2 for the carrying pull request runs on `codex` (option label: "Codex (Recommended)"). **Not merge authorization:** `/04-agents/` and `/07-decisions/` are `DEC-20260814-02` trigger surfaces; the carrying pull request merges only after independent Tier-2 of its exact final head and the Founder's separate SHA-named authorization under `DEC-20260718-04`. This log advances `v4.96` → `v4.97`.

**v4.96 (2026-10-24) — `DEC-20260807-01` Amendment 1: when the CGER record store counts as live, and no back-recording of executions before it; drafted by `builder` at the Founder's direction and ratified by the Founder; plus two Founder rulings of 2026-10-24 recorded here from `HO-20261024-01`.** **Why:** every G3 Slice B+ build to date is a governed coding execution under clause 2 with no persisted record, because the store's migrations are merged but not applied to the hosted Neon. The Founder selected "No backfill; records start at activation" (option label and text recorded in the decision's Amendment 1 section), and agreed, verbatim "I agree", to the builder's four drafting recommendations recorded there. The amendment adds item 7 to clause 2 (proposed at `e000735`, corrected at `af10edb` on Copilot's review, ratified at `af10edb`, carried into the clause by the next commit): the store is live from the first execution after migrations `1784419200007`–`1784419200011` are applied to the hosted Neon default branch (evidenced by `pgmigrations`) **and** the runtime's own tooling has performed one successful write and read-back of a `cger_execution_records` row (the contract's write/read reconciliation), with the evidence locator recorded; earlier executions are not back-recorded, and their evidence of record is the merge commit with trailers, the pull request body with the item 2 fields, the Founder's merge-authorization comment, and the review-evidence bundle with its `VERDICT.md` digest. **Two further rulings of 2026-10-24, recorded here as their canonical log entry:** (a) the pre-pilot implementation baseline for G3 Slice B+ is `founder-os-telegram` `main` `c16e7ff6ea67ec225c9d322874928e50a005e385` (Founder selection "Closed at c16e7ff6"); later work is tracked as the named remainder in `HO-20261024-01` (RP-6/RP-7 supervisor, T-22–T-26, the two hardening migrations, fixture refresh F-1, executor and counted run, MP-1); this decision defines pilot stages and implementation verification, not a "build phase", and none is declared complete by this entry. (b) The grok-served Tier-2 reviews of `founder-os-telegram` pull requests #132, #133 and #134 loaded the private repository with no prior per-invocation `DEC-20260716-01` clause-6 authorization; the Founder's merge-authorization comments (`6335801162`, `6340179443`, `6345403852`) were posted after those reviews ran. Those three reviews were therefore **non-compliant with `DEC-20260716-01` clause 6 at the time they ran**, and `DEC-20260719-02` lists "waiving data-boundary rules for Grok on internal-class material" among the things it does not do, so no instrument short of a Founder amendment to those decisions could make them compliant after the fact. The Founder selected "My merge authorizations covered it"; recorded here as the Founder's **retroactive acceptance of the risk** for those three completed reviews, in the Founder's authority over the Founder's own repositories' exposure, and nothing more: this entry amends neither decision, converts the reviews into compliant ones, nor sets a precedent. Every later grok-served review of internal-class material requires the prior per-invocation authorization, as pull requests #135 and #136 carried and as the `grok-4.5` row in `DEC-20260719-02`'s Decision item 2 states (that row cites `DEC-20260716-01` clause 6; `DEC-20260719-02`'s own clause 6 is the independence rule). If the Founder wants the acceptance itself to carry decision-level authority, it needs its own amendment to `DEC-20260716-01` or `DEC-20260719-02`; none is proposed here. **Founder ratification of Amendment 1, 2026-10-24:** comment 6389927395 on FounderOS pull request 398, recorded verbatim in the decision; it ratifies the Amendment 1 section at head `af10edb67dda3169492c7e55b090785cd2a1a1c9` (file SHA-256 `44af5a6d2cd52528ce79e1287090490e1fc2082394f15fbdd1492c0c03509b3c`) and nothing beyond it, effective on merge, and authorizes no executor, counted run, deployment, merge or spend. Item 7 is now in clause 2. `DEC-20260807-01` advances `v0.3` → `v0.5` (`v0.4` is the ratified head `af10edb`). **Not merge authorization:** `/07-decisions/` is a `DEC-20260814-02` trigger surface; the carrying pull request merges only after the Founder's ratification, independent Tier-2 of its exact final head and the Founder's separate SHA-named authorization under `DEC-20260718-04`. **v4.76 (2026-09-29) — the `attribution-shape` checker made to match git's divider exactly and to reject descriptions that GitHub's merge wrap would break; a technical enforcement of two existing rules, executed at Founder direction.** Record-keeping of executed build work under `DEC-20260814-03` clause 3; no new decision, no identifier allocated, nothing superseded, no new doctrine. **Founder direction, recorded verbatim** (session 2026-09-29): "a technical enforcement of existing rules (the 72-character trailer bound and \"no divider above the trailers\"), executed at Founder direction of 2026-09-29, with no new doctrine". The two rules are the 72-character attribution-trailer bound (`CLAUDE.md`; v4.45) and "no divider above the trailers" (`CLAUDE.md`; v4.70). **Freeze posture.** `DEC-20260814-03` is `active`, window 2026-08-17 through 2026-11-14 inclusive; named check: `builder` searched this log, `09-handoffs/handoff-index.md` and `07-decisions/ops-actions-log.md` on 2026-09-29 for a ruling that lifts, waives or ends it and found none. Its clause 2 freezes new governance machinery and process refinements. The Founder's reading, given in session 2026-09-29 and recorded verbatim: "Proceed, on your reading: my 2026-09-29 direction is a clause-2 authorization notwithstanding the freeze, as in v4.45. Record it that way, quote v4.45, and claim no clause-4 blocked ship." v4.45 quotes the Founder ruling of 2026-08-30 that this rests on: "I authorize the proposed attribution trailer line-length safeguard as a narrow clause-2 process refinement notwithstanding the current freeze." It continues, on enforcement before merge: "It may, however, enforce this source-line-length safeguard before merge so known-overlength attribution lines do not enter the affected merge path." And on the freeze: "It does not invoke clause 4, does not identify or require a blocked ship, and does not otherwise relax the freeze." This row claims the same footing and no more: **no clause-4 exception is claimed and no blocked ship is identified.** By its own text the 2026-08-30 ruling covers pre-merge enforcement of the 72-character bound; the divider character set and the wide-line rule below rest on the 2026-09-29 direction alone. **What changed.** `00-system/scripts/attribution-shape-check.sh` only; no workflow file, because `.github/workflows/attribution-shape.yml` already passes the body raw and already runs the proposed checker's `prbody` mode, `pr` mode and selftest on the pull request itself. (1) One divider pattern, `^---([[:blank:]]|\r|$)` under `LC_ALL=C`, now serves the commit-message check (`validate_block`), the PR title and the PR body, matched on the text before any CR is stripped. A line is a git divider when it begins with `---` and the character immediately after the hyphens is a space, a tab or a CR, or the line ends there; text may follow, so `--- x` is a divider and `---x` and `----` are not. The previous `[[:space:]]` class also matched a vertical tab, a form feed and, under UTF-8, U+3000, all of which git reads past, and matching after the CR strip let `---` CR `x` pass while git stopped reading at it. (2) Two rules in `prbody` mode for GitHub's merge wrap: in a line wider than 72 columns, a `---` bounded on each side by the line's edge or a byte that is not visible ASCII fails; and an attribution line wider than 72 columns fails. Width expands a tab to the next multiple of 8 and counts every other byte as one column, under `LC_ALL=C`; a CR counts as one column, except the CR that ends a CRLF line, and the trailer rule reads the raw body. (3) Selftest 81 to 118 cases. Ported from `MADVenturesLLC/founder-os-build-room` (`main` at `25b2d8448f8ac4506ae70e078a80a365a20fedb4`): commits `2f94a9c60c4fe1942bf42e325d2fd8d7566897aa` (divider character set), `813a1903c62a2c64380b4c25a3649d40a74a6d91` (merge-wrap rules) and `c6628dbfb9261f2134f3d4ce879820eef56bdd7b` (the wrap rules by width, its Tier-2 FAIL fix), with commit-mode cases, 72/73-column boundary cases and, after review, three CR cases added here. **Measured before porting, and stated with its limits.** Named check: `builder`, 2026-09-29, each pull request's final description read with `gh api repos/MADVenturesLLC/FounderOS/pulls/{n}` and compared byte for byte with the merge commit read from `origin/main` at `642f65180429131563d029ca3dc3137c209d6843`. Of the 279 merge commits whose subject ends in "(#N)", 226 reproduce under one model and 223 of those differ from the description as written: a line longer than 72 characters outside a fenced code block loses its indent and is broken greedily at whitespace, and fenced lines are left alone (as v4.45 records). That includes the two merges `DEC-20260721-01` records as made through the REST API, `aa715a3` (PR #130) and `25e60e5` (PR #203), each byte for byte. Of the other 53, 46 carry messages that are subject-only, short or abridged rather than the description (median 5 lines; the longest, 73 lines against 144 expected), 6 differ by one hunk (two descriptions that appear to have been edited after merge, four messages with extra blank or separator lines) and 1 is as written. Whether a given merge went through the API is not observable from the commit; only the two merges above are documented as such. Unmeasured, and taken as the wider reading: how GitHub handles a tab, vertical tab, form feed or U+3000, none of which occurs in any of the 279 descriptions. Git's divider was probed on git 2.54.0 (Apple Git-157) with ten endings after `---`; Build Room measured git 2.43.0; the git on the `ubuntu-latest` runner was not measured here and is exercised by the pull request's own checks. **Replay.** Old checker (`origin/main`) and new, `prbody` mode, description = message minus its first two lines and `PR_TITLE` = subject, over all 308 first-parent merge commits on `origin/main`: 248 pass and 60 fail under both, **0 verdicts change**, and `main` mode over the same 308 gives 250 pass and 58 fail under both, 0 changes, under `LC_ALL=C` and `C.UTF-8`. That is expected, and measured rather than assumed (named check: `builder`, 2026-09-29, over the same 308 messages): none contains a tab, vertical tab, form feed, U+3000 or CR, none has an attribution line wider than 72 columns, and none has a wide line holding a standalone `---`. A supplementary replay over the 279 final descriptions as the pre-merge gate saw them changes **one** verdict: PR #316 (`24cd66a`), whose 74-character `Actor-Id` line is the incident v4.45 records, now fails before merge. Nothing else changes. Both replays were re-run after the CR fix with the same results. **Mutations, run and not committed** (22 mutants, each proven different from the pristine script, control 0 failures): 18 killed, by named selftest cases: the old `[[:space:]]` class (5 cases), title and body matched after CR strip (1 each), each of the three divider sites dropped (1 each), width counted as bytes in either rule or both (1, 1, 2), the old space-or-tab break class (4), a `>73` bound in either rule (2 each), each merge-wrap rule dropped (11 and 4), and four CR mutants: the wide-line rule deleting every CR (1), the trailer rule deleting every CR (1), the trailer rule reading the CR-stripped copy of the attribution lines (1) and the trailer rule counting the CR that ends a CRLF line (1). Four survive. (i) Unpinned locale survives on macOS, where BSD grep does not treat U+3000 as blank in `C.UTF-8`; the advisor session measured it killed on Linux (git 2.43.0, GNU grep 3.11, 2 cases), as the Founder relayed, and `builder` did not reproduce that. (ii) Matching the commit message after a CR strip survives because the `git interpret-trailers` check in `validate_block` rejects the same messages, so only the failure text differs, and the selftest compares exit codes. (iii) The wide-line rule counting the CR that ends a CRLF line survives: no case puts a `---` in a CRLF line of exactly 72 columns. (iv) Removing the `|| true` after the trailer rule's `grep` survives because the exit code is 1 either way; measured on a body with no attribution line, the checker then prints no reason at all. **Pipeline race, folded in at Founder direction.** Founder direction, recorded verbatim (session 2026-09-29): "fold the race fix into 361". Ten `printf ... | grep -q` pipelines in the checker are replaced by here-strings: the commit-mode and `prbody` membership checks, the pure-block check, the `pr`-mode applicability check, the `Role-Id` and `Role-Id-Secondary` value checks in both modes, and the two branch checks. One selftest guard case fails if a piped `grep -q` returns, which takes the selftest from 117 to 118 cases. **Measured by the advisor session, not by `builder`,** on Linux (bash 5.2, GNU grep 3.11). Under `set -o pipefail`, `printf` piped into `grep -q` is killed by SIGPIPE (exit 141) when `grep` exits at its first match. Under parallel load that gave 40 false results in 24,000 runs; a here-string gave 0 in 24,000. About 5 false rejections appeared in about 360 full selftest runs of this checker; one commit-mode case may instead have been a signing-helper fault in the advisor session's container. By site: the two membership checks give false reds (fail-closed); the `pr`-mode applicability check and the pure-block check could, in principle, miss a violation (fail-open); the single-line checks were not affected. The same ten pipelines are on `main` at `642f651`, so they are not new in this pull request (named check: `builder` counted them in the script at `642f651` and at `1827d6c` with the guard's own pattern on 2026-09-29, ten at each, and none after the change). The Build Room and `madventures-tui` copies carry them too; those are follow-ups, not this pull request. The advisor session reports that the v4.72 replay is unaffected; `builder` did not re-run either replay for this change. `builder` did not reproduce the race. It applied the advisor session's diff (`race-fix-361.diff`, SHA-256 `4e33a4135a680c847d0521de136af671f1124f4d8999abd48c161084d54e87c2`) to `1827d6c` and measured on macOS (bash 3.2.57, BSD grep 2.6.0): selftest 117 to 118 cases with exit 0, and the guard case failing, exit 1, on a copy of the script with one piped `grep -q` restored. The 22-mutant suite above ran at `1827d6c` and was not re-run for this change. **Review and Tier-2.** Copilot review 5350706867, on the first head `a5f29a5`, returned one Medium and three Low findings, all taken in a second push. Medium: the wide-line rule and the trailer rule deleted every CR before measuring width, but the wrap model breaks at a CR, so 69 letters, a CR and `---` (73 bytes) passed the wide-line rule and a 72-column attribution line with a mid-line CR (73 bytes) passed the trailer rule. Both rules now count a mid-line CR as one column and drop only the CR that ends a CRLF line, and the trailer rule reads the raw body in place of the CR-stripped copy. The advisor session reproduced both; `builder` ran the same two bodies through the checker at `a5f29a5` (exit 0 for both) and at the fix (exit 1 for both). Three selftest cases pin it. Low, in three places: the divider wording in the checker comment, in this row and in `CLAUDE.md` said or implied that nothing may follow the space, tab or CR, when `--- x` is a divider; each now says the character right after `---` decides and text may follow. Tier-2 review (gemini-3.1-pro) returned PASS at `a5f29a5`, as the Founder reported in session 2026-09-29. This fix moves the head, which voids that marker under `DEC-20260814-02`, and a delta re-review of the new head follows. The race fix moves the head again; Tier-2 review of the exact head after it is pending, and its prompt comes from the advisor session. **Doctrine text corrected.** One statement in `CLAUDE.md` became inaccurate and is edited: the Cursor Bugbot bullet said git treats `---` followed by "whitespace or end-of-line" as end-of-message. It now says git treats a line as a divider when the character right after `---` is a space, a tab or a CR, or the line ends there, and that text may follow. The first version of this edit added "(and no other character)", which Copilot found wrong for `--- x`; that is removed. `CLAUDE.md` version 1.4 to 1.6. Nothing else in `CLAUDE.md` or `00-system/scripts/README.md` is made inaccurate by this change. **Version number, disclosed.** v4.72 is taken because pull request #359 ("decisions: v4.72 records DEC-20260814-01 deferral; refresh stale citations") also claims v4.72 and was `OPEN`, unmerged, when this row was written (named check: `gh pr view 359` by `builder`, 2026-09-29T08:27Z). Whichever pull request merges second renumbers its row. Renumbered from v4.72 to v4.76 on 2026-09-30 at rebase onto `033a633b` (this pull request being first of the two claimants to reach merge-ready, holding the shared number through merge would have relied on merge order to avoid a duplicate row; PR #359's claim to v4.72 is left standing). `v4.75` is allocated to the pending green-lane activation entry; numbers are allocations, not merge-order guarantees (the v4.12/4.13 precedent). **Asserts no live-system state.** **Not merge authorization:** `00-system/scripts/` and `/07-decisions/` are `DEC-20260814-02` trigger surfaces, so the carrying pull request requires independent Tier-2 review of its exact final head and a separate SHA-named Founder authorization under `DEC-20260718-04`. **v4.71 (2026-09-27) — `MADVenturesLLC/founder-os-build-room` made public, and the organization's Actions billing settings changed, by Founder act; recorded here, not decided here.** Record-keeping of executed Founder acts under `DEC-20260814-03` clause 3; no new decision, no identifier allocated, nothing superseded. **Founder direction, recorded verbatim** (session 2026-09-27): "going to put the repo public", given after "my action minutes are used up 2k/2k"; then "added a card to the org, check again" and "removed the $0 budget, check again". **(1) The repository is public.** Named check: GitHub API repository read by `builder` at 2026-09-27T02:35Z; visibility `public`, repository `updated_at` 02:26:03Z. **(2) Going public did not by itself restore hosted Actions.** Named check: workflow-job reads by `builder` between 02:36Z and 04:04Z; every job was created as a real job, then failed within about two seconds with no runner assigned (`runner_id` 0, no log). The Founder reported that GitHub's message on those jobs said the spending limit needed to be increased. **(3) The Founder changed the organization's billing settings:** a payment card was added and a $0 budget was removed. These are the Founder's reports; `builder` has no billing read access and has not verified them. **(4) Hosted Actions recovered.** Named check: workflow-job read by `builder`; the first job to get a GitHub-hosted runner started at 2026-09-27T04:06:10Z (`founder-os-build-room` CI run `36288984538`, attempt 8), and a run at 04:04Z had still failed. Which change restored runner assignment is not established, and this row does not attribute it. **Why.** Every GitHub Actions run created in the repository from 2026-09-26T17:11:15Z until the flip at 02:26:03Z, 27 runs, concluded `startup_failure`; the run before that window, at 2026-09-25T23:04:38Z, had succeeded (named check: workflow-run listing read by `builder` at 2026-09-27T10:41Z). The Founder attributed this to the organization's exhausted 2,000-minute Actions quota. Standard GitHub-hosted runners are free in public repositories (GitHub Docs, *GitHub Actions billing*, read 2026-09-27), yet jobs stayed blocked after the flip until the billing changes, so the quota alone is not established as the cause. The repository's hosted checks did not run for [Build Room PR #75](https://github.com/MADVenturesLLC/founder-os-build-room/pull/75), which merged at [`41de91d9a61b6c4453570370adff60dc005be32c`](https://github.com/MADVenturesLLC/founder-os-build-room/commit/41de91d9a61b6c4453570370adff60dc005be32c) without them; that repository's correction note, C4, records this and is on its `main` through [Build Room PR #77](https://github.com/MADVenturesLLC/founder-os-build-room/pull/77) (merge [`9fda98771a3016f96e45b707e1228483c885e1d8`](https://github.com/MADVenturesLLC/founder-os-build-room/commit/9fda98771a3016f96e45b707e1228483c885e1d8)). **PR #75's merge is dispositioned separately.** The Founder has chosen `NONCOMPLIANT_MERGE_RECORDED` in a disposition drafted 2026-09-27 for signature, to land in `founder-os-build-room` at `docs/planning/command-journal/custody/FOUNDER-RULING-pr75-ci-gate-disposition-20260927.txt`; it was not issued when this row was written, and this row neither issues nor restates it. **A consequence this log should carry.** GitHub's documentation (*Deployments and environments* reference, read 2026-09-27) says that on the Free, Pro and Team plans required reviewers are available only for public repositories, and that on Free, environment secrets are available only in public repositories. `DEC-20260917-01` records the Build Room administrative custody domain as an environment-scoped secret behind required reviewers `decivantiq` and `daley40-lab`. Whether that posture needs the repository to be public depends on the organization's plan, which has not been read from billing. [Likely] the plan is Free, since the 2,000-minute quota is the Free-plan allowance; if so, the posture could not be configured while the repository was private, and public visibility is what makes it, and therefore Gate IV, configurable, whatever restored CI. The plan is unconfirmed, and this row does not establish that Gate IV depends on the flip. This row neither creates the environment nor issues Gate IV. **Exposure accepted with the visibility act:** (a) the production control-plane base URL, in six committed Phase 2 evidence bundles (`evidence/phase2-runs-*.json`); (b) two personal email addresses in the author field of 108 commits on `main` at the flip (`41de91d`), and of 109 at `9fda987`, whose merge commit carries one of them; removing them would mean rewriting history that custody records pin by SHA, and they have not been removed; (c) the full governance, custody and ruling record; (d) the reviewer account names. Named check for (a) and (b): `git grep` and `git log` over `founder-os-build-room` `main` = `9fda98771a3016f96e45b707e1228483c885e1d8` by `builder` at 2026-09-27T11:00Z. **Spend exposure accepted with the billing act.** [Likely] With the $0 budget removed and a card on file, Actions usage in the organization's private repositories (`FounderOS`, `madventures-tui`) beyond the included quota now bills to that card; whether another budget caps it is not known to `builder`. [Guessing] A budget that stops usage when reached may block public-repository jobs again, as the removed $0 budget appears to have done. **Checked before the visibility act:** the repository's secret scan (`gate:secret-scan`, gitleaks 8.30.1) found no leaks across its history on 2026-09-26; and both workflows that hold model credentials under `pull_request_target` (`claude-code-review.yml`, `advisory-reviewers.yml`, read at `main` = `41de91d9a61b6c4453570370adff60dc005be32c`) gate their credential-holding jobs on same-repository, human-authored pull requests, so a pull request from a fork does not reach the secrets. **Left to the Founder, not decided here:** confirming the organization's GitHub plan; an Actions budget or alert for the organization; the strength and rotation of the control plane's API token, now that its URL is public; the repository's "require approval for first-time contributors" Actions setting; the visibility of `FounderOS` and `madventures-tui`. **What this does not change.** The row that advanced this log to v4.17 (the WF-04 Step 3 Slice 1 entry) records the repository as created "private"; that describes its state at creation and stays accurate for that date. `DEC-20260815-01` contains no visibility clause, so no ruling is superseded. **Not merge authorization.** `DEC-20260822-01` clause 1's global exclusions cover a change that alters security posture. This row records a security-posture change and a billing change rather than making them, so whether it falls inside that exclusion is arguable, and clause 1 has uncertainty fail closed. The carrying pull request therefore takes the normal governed path: independent Tier-2 review of its exact final head and a separate SHA-named Founder authorization under `DEC-20260718-04`. Recorded by `builder` (`session:claude-code/br-repo-visibility`, surface `claude-code`) at Founder direction. decision-log v4.70 → v4.71.

**v4.70 (2026-09-21) — three gate defects closed at Founder direction; two doctrine texts corrected; one index gap back-filled.** Record-keeping of executed build work under `DEC-20260814-03` clause 3; no new decision, no identifier allocated, nothing superseded. Executes items the v4.69 row left for Founder disposition, ruled by the Founder in session 2026-09-21 ("Let's do 1-5 while I'm still in port"). **(1) `prbody` now rejects a git message divider anywhere in the body.** `git interpret-trailers` reads trailers only from what precedes a line beginning with `---` followed by whitespace or end-of-line; the end-anchored gate could not see one above the block. Measured on PR #355 at 13:58Z: gate PASS, zero trailers parsed. `00-system/scripts/attribution-shape-check.sh` `prbody` mode now fails and names the line; eight selftest cases pin git's documented rule (`---`, `--- `, `--- x`, and a fenced `---` rejected; `----`, `---x`, `***`, indented ` ---` accepted), verified by experiment against git 2.43.0. **(2) `validate_block` now requires `Execution-Surface` wherever `Role-Id` is present**, closing FounderOS issue #354: `prbody` enforced it and the commit path never read it, so the authoritative record under `DEC-20260718-05` clause 7 accepted what the body gate rejected. Two `st_commit` cases pin it. Both gate paths now enforce the field unconditionally when a role is attested; whether the contract wording "when known" should change to match is the Founder's and is not decided here. Copilot review of PR #356 raised two findings, both dispositioned in the same pull request: (a) High — the unconditional `Execution-Surface` requirement is stricter than the contract wording "recommended always and required where known" (`agent-rules.md` §11.1; `DEC-20260718-05` clause 6). The reading applied, now pinned by selftest in all three modes: the gate requires the line with a non-empty value, not a registry-known surface, and `unknown` — the value `DEC-20260718-05`'s clause-9 template and the PR template themselves give for a surface that is not known — satisfies it; so "required where known" is met by writing `unknown` when it is not. That is the reading, not a ruling; the wording question above stays the Founder's. (b) Medium — the regression cases reached `validate_block` only through `main`; `st_pr_applicable` now exercises the `pr` applicable-commit path with five cases (valid; no block; missing surface; empty surface; `unknown`), measured RED on the `main` checker and GREEN on this one. Copilot's second round raised two further High findings, both taken: (c) `.github/workflows/path-audit.yml` ran the pull request's OWN `path-audit.sh`, so a pull request could replace the script with `exit 0` and receive a green required check — measured: the old step exits 0 on a tree carrying a no-op script and a dangling path reference; the step now takes the script from the base revision exactly as `attribution-shape.yml` selects its checker (`git ls-tree`, regular-file modes only, fail closed on any read error, bootstrap only when genuinely absent) and runs it against the pull request tree, a step change under `DEC-20260718-02` clause 4 on the same approval route as the trigger change (`e31934fd5125bd2cbee8618fe76189823438a3a5`); (d) the divider rule was scoped to `prbody` while `validate_block` still grepped raw commit messages, so a commit carrying `---` above its block passed `pr` and `main` while `git interpret-trailers --parse` read nothing; `validate_block` now applies the divider rule and, in both it and `prbody`, asks git directly — every attribution key the greps find must come back from `git interpret-trailers --parse` (`69292d7c706909d7ab042441766762d4af5c8cbb`). **Finding for Founder disposition, recorded and not repaired:** run in `main` mode over the last twelve commits on `main`, that rule rejects two already-merged commits — `ec6a51383f14d1438ba5235990e491f03ee1b1e1` (2026-09-17; Cursor Bugbot's block with its bare `---` sits at line 107 of the message, above the trailers) and `151edd483c644ef5d47203454ecdc9d64116c5f2` (2026-09-18; a bare `---` at line 382). On both, `git interpret-trailers --parse` returns zero trailers, so the authoritative record under `DEC-20260718-05` clause 7 holds each block as text that git does not read as trailers; the checker on `main` passes both, which is the blind spot this row closes. Published history is not rewritten; what, if anything, is done about those two records is the Founder's. Copilot's third round raised one further High finding, taken: (e) the `prbody` parse prepended a constant subject, but the merge commit's first line is the PR title, and a title beginning with `---` followed by whitespace or end-of-line is a divider above everything — measured: title `--- x`, fully valid body, zero trailers parse, gate green. `prbody` now reads the title from `PR_TITLE`, rejects a divider-shaped one, and parses under the real title, falling back to the synthetic subject when unset (`7220db09d8b30eb371445204b22f2e4d859d3b29`); `.github/workflows/attribution-shape.yml` passes the title as an env mapping alongside the body, a step change on the same approval route (`738295843fdcbb8a06c758fbaf6f70cae08f8878`). Copilot's fourth round raised two findings, both taken: (f) High — the git-readability check compared key names only while the validated values were still grepped from the raw message, so a commit with `Actor-Id: founder` in a prose paragraph and a parsed `Actor-Id: session:x` with no `Role-Id` satisfied the direct-founder branch (reproduced: exit 0 in `main` and `pr` mode); `validate_block` now takes every value from `git interpret-trailers --parse` and rejects any raw attribution-shaped line git does not read, value included, and `prbody` applies the same line rule (`e949023c598dd157d4ec81442aaa8ccfa2d59194`); (g) Low — `CLAUDE.md`'s frontmatter had not moved with its guidance change; `updated:` 2026-09-21, `version:` 1.3 (`39f26401baf025b9f31ebac079573e58f192f825`). Copilot's fifth round raised one High finding, taken: (h) the body gate runs the checker copied from the base revision, so every hardening — the title guard included — is enforced only from the NEXT pull request; nothing a pull request carries can be trusted on that pull request, so the lag is closed by enforcing more, not trusting more: `.github/workflows/attribution-shape.yml` now also runs the PROPOSED checker in `pr` and `prbody` mode as a final, enforcing step, which cannot weaken the gate (the base copy's verdicts stand) and holds the introducing pull request to its own rules — measured: this pull request passes both modes under its own checker and fails under a divider-shaped title, while the base copy passes that title; a step change on the same approval route (`1201c70de6de8a8b08bdb82be92dc27811d60250`). Copilot's sixth round raised one Medium finding, taken: (i) the per-line check tested set membership, so a stray line duplicating a real trailer passed; the raw attribution-shaped lines and the trailers git parses must now be the same multiset, in both paths (`376fc0117a6e5d00b7261a0e6489c010ea4c926a`). Copilot's seventh round raised one High finding, taken: (j) the enforcing final step ran the proposed checker from the working tree after PR-controlled code had run there, so a self-test that replaced the file with a no-op would have been executed and passed; the step now takes the checker from the head commit object immediately before use — measured: with the on-disk checker replaced by `exit 0` and a divider-shaped title, the step fails and names the title, where the previous form exited 0 (`f6b83f919eb63f24bb39f6f4a0c914dad7e76b1c`). Copilot's eighth round raised six findings: four taken — (k) the proposed self-test ran before the enforcing step and could have altered the environment, so the two PR-controlled steps are reordered with the enforcing step first; (l) the head extraction did not check the tree entry's mode, so a symlink at the path would have yielded its target text — regular-file modes only now, fail closed; (m) the first invocation could rewrite the temporary file before the second — re-materialised per invocation (`e9309a73e72f7fe872f70cf6f9c8641f2919cb47`); (n) `path-audit.yml` had no proposed-revision run — it now also runs the pull request's own script from the head object (`14aed18904399f20da3fb8951bd52fb5c69e1410`); (o) Low — `CLAUDE.md`'s re-read instruction now says to parse the exact merge message, title plus body, and names the title divider; version 1.4 (`4f46a3192b9c883e070f1bad1ac31a4ca3802c73`); and two **declined on the record**, both stating that a pull request which weakens its own checker or audit script passes the base copy, passes itself, and once merged becomes the base copy for later pull requests — true, and true of the trusted-copy design as such since `DEC-20260721-01`, not of any step this pull request adds; the control for it is review and the Founder's SHA-named authorization under `DEC-20260718-04`, now stated as the threat model in both workflow headers. Copilot's ninth round raised one High finding, taken in both workflows: (p) the bootstrap branch — taken only when the base revision genuinely lacks the script — copied it from the working tree with `cp`, which follows symlinks; both workflows now take the bootstrap copy from the head commit object after the same regular-file mode check, unreachable on this repository where both scripts exist at base (`925d80be8fef24ff4f53b63a1419e65ef4d2678f`, `1d639302710ff7da9b7ed631cdd24dd89670709e`). Selftest 41 → 81. **(3) `.github/workflows/path-audit.yml` no longer filters on `paths: ['**.md']`.** A required check with a paths filter is unsatisfiable on any diff the filter excludes; PR #355 could merge only after a Markdown change was added to it. Trigger change under `DEC-20260718-02` clause 4, Founder-directed; the SHA-named merge authorization for the carrying pull request is that approval. What the audit checks is unchanged. **(4) `CLAUDE.md` appender guidance corrected:** Cursor Bugbot recorded as the fifth body appender; the remedy amended from "relocate above the trailers" — which measurably produced a green gate over a merge commit with no parseable trailers — to "relocate AND neutralise the bare `---` as `***`"; the pre-authorization re-read now names `git interpret-trailers --parse` as the check. **(5) v4.68 index pointer back-filled** — see that row. **Downstream:** `MADVenturesLLC/madventures-tui` PR #95 ports the symlink allowlist from PR #355 into that repository's workflow; the checker re-vendor there follows this merge, per that repository's byte-identity rule. **Asserts no live-system state.** **Not merge authorization:** `DEC-20260718-04` is unchanged; `.github/workflows/` and `/07-decisions/` are `DEC-20260814-02` trigger surfaces, so the carrying pull request requires independent Tier-2 review of its exact final head and a separate SHA-named Founder authorization. Recorded by `builder` (`session:claude-code/ls-tree-fix`, surface `claude-code`) at Founder direction. decision-log v4.69 → v4.70.

**v4.69 (2026-09-21) — two fail-opens closed in the `attribution-shape` trusted-checker selection; two gate defects found and NOT closed.** Record-keeping of executed build work under `DEC-20260814-03` clause 3; no new decision, no identifier allocated, no rule created, nothing superseded. **What was executed.** `FounderOS` PR #355 (head `9f021becbc91a54e3269f8c686ac250e1d956d52`, base `main` = `9d4ab792b64daf4ea909fcda30fbbc04677a68ad`) changes `.github/workflows/attribution-shape.yml` only; `00-system/scripts/attribution-shape-check.sh` is untouched at `sha256 ebda0fa2c0fa52e3b946fa3aa8b25d3f0096ae32bdc5e0471eba41718c20494e`. Commit `37b0b1097e0de5a3a08500d29d5fa199a2cad883` replaced `git cat-file -e` with `git ls-tree` in both trusted-checker steps: `cat-file` conflates "path not in the tree" with "blob unreadable", so a readable base tree with a missing or corrupt checker blob satisfied the `^{tree}` probe and copied the PULL REQUEST's own script into the trusted slot that the body gate then executes. Commit `9f021becbc91a54e3269f8c686ac250e1d956d52` closed a hole in that fix raised by Copilot at `#discussion_r4062691494`: `ls-tree` reports a symlink as `120000 blob`, so matching `*" blob "*` accepted one and `git show` wrote the link TARGET TEXT into the trusted slot — a target of `:` yields a one-byte shell no-op that exits 0, so the gate passes without running. Accepted modes are now `100644`/`100755` only. **Measured, not asserted:** each step's own `run:` body was extracted from the YAML and executed under `bash -e`; the seven-case matrix (regular file, executable, base unreadable, blob unreadable, genuinely absent, tree entry, symlink) is recorded in both commit messages and the PR body. On `main` the blob-unreadable case returned exit 0 with the PR copy installed and the symlink case took the bootstrap path; both now fail closed. **Provenance:** the `ls-tree` form was reviewed and merged first downstream at `MADVenturesLLC/madventures-tui` PR #92 (merge commit `75a059b8ffcdc2af79f866429530ec626ec6f432`), where the finding arose against the vendored copy; this brings the merge-blocking copy into line. **The symlink hole remains live downstream** in that repository and is not addressed by PR #355. **Two gate defects found and deliberately NOT closed here, both for Founder disposition.** (1) `prbody` cannot detect the failure it exists to prevent: it is END-anchored, verifying only that nothing FOLLOWS the trailers, while `git interpret-trailers` treats a bare three-hyphen rule as end-of-message, so a `---` ABOVE the trailers silently strips every trailer from the resulting merge commit. Measured on PR #355 with Cursor Bugbot's summary block placed above the trailers exactly as `CLAUDE.md`'s stated remedy prescribes: `prbody` PASS exit 0, `git interpret-trailers --parse` returns ZERO trailers — a green gate over a broken artifact, which is the `madventures-tui` `c3c7d9fe21036b8d90c9d2bf0feabf5e7d9df624` outcome reached by following the written procedure. Nothing in CI runs `git interpret-trailers --parse` against a pull-request body. The PR #355 body neutralizes the rule as `***` (identical rendering, not a divider, no word of the summary altered) and says so on its face; `CLAUDE.md` is NOT amended by this row. (2) `path-audit` is a required status check on `main` whose workflow triggers on `paths: ['**.md']`, so it cannot fire on a code-only or CI-only diff and sits at "Expected" indefinitely — a required check with a paths filter is unsatisfiable on every diff the filter excludes. PR #353 was unaffected because it carried `09-handoffs/` Markdown, not because it touched this log, which it did not. **Housekeeping observed, not corrected:** commit `fbcdd66` advanced this file's frontmatter `version: 4.67` → `4.68` without adding a v4.68 row, so no v4.68 entry exists; this row is numbered v4.69 to avoid reusing a consumed number, and the gap is left for the Founder rather than back-filled. **Asserts no live-system state:** no claim is made here about branch-protection or ruleset configuration, which was not read; the `path-audit` finding rests on the workflow file's own trigger block and on `DEC-20260801-02` clause 1. **Not merge authorization:** `DEC-20260718-04` is unchanged and a SHA-named, Founder-authored authorization naming the pull request and its exact head remains required; `.github/workflows/` is a `DEC-20260814-02` trigger surface, so PR #355 also requires independent Tier-2 review of its exact final head. Recorded by `builder` (`session:claude-code/ls-tree-fix`, surface `claude-code`) at Founder direction. decision-log v4.68 → v4.69.

**v4.68 (2026-09-17) — index pointer, back-filled 2026-09-21: the version advanced without a row.** Commit `fbcdd663de3715ff3fd07794eb3aeac54e19da0f` (`reconcile(decisions): DEC-20260913-03 v0.2 -> v0.3 record-only (citations + status line)`) advanced this file's frontmatter `version: 4.67` → `4.68` and `updated:` to 2026-09-17 and added no row, so no v4.68 entry existed and the next row was allocated as v4.69 past the gap. This row is the index pointer only, back-filled at Founder direction so the sequence is contiguous and followable from the log; the substance of that commit lives in `DEC-20260913-03` v0.3 and is not restated here. **v4.67 (2026-09-18) — `DEC-20260917-01` ratified `active`: the recording instrument for the PR 2b administrative custody domain is a decision record, the domain itself is the GitHub protected environment `db-admin-migration`, and PR 2b `FD-2` is discharged.** Discharges **PR 2b `FD-2`** — the item at `pr2b-implementation-plan-r1.md` §12, not Baseline v4.0 §`FD-2` (retention/backup/recovery) and not the Fable package v1.0 `FD-2` cited at `DEC-20260815-02` (evidence-store custody); the label is overloaded and is qualified here for that reason. Ratified `source_of_truth: true`: the record is the domain of record and `maintenance-rules` §4 rule 1 admits exactly one such claimant per subject, with no other file claiming this one. **Founder ruling, recorded verbatim:** "ratify the DEC" — issued in session 2026-09-18 against the `proposed` v0.1 draft of 2026-09-17. **The substance lives in `DEC-20260917-01`, not in this row.** It records the GitHub protected environment `db-admin-migration` in `MADVenturesLLC/founder-os-build-room` as the custody domain for the Build Room administrative database credential, with the §4.3 C-X1–C-X3 control posture (environment-scoped secret only; required reviewers `decivantiq` and `daley40-lab`; `prevent_self_review` on; administrator bypass off; deployment branch policy restricted to `main`; no wait timer), the §4.3 FORBIDDEN placements as exclusive, and future custody changes as Founder acts landing by amendment to that file. **What it does not decide.** The custody move itself was already ratified at `pr2b-storage-architecture-r6.md` §19 under `DEC-20260815-02`; PR 2b `FD-2` asked only which instrument records it, and a decision record was chosen over a Founder authorization comment for durability, consistency with `DEC-20260815-02`, and findability in `/07-decisions/`. Tranche C is configuration-only — §4.3: "Database objects affected by C: none. Railway configuration affected: none." **Asserts no live-system state:** the file states the intended configuration and records no check of the live GitHub environment; `C-T1` and `PC-28`/`PC-29` are the verification, and the recorded posture goes stale silently if the environment is reconfigured without amending the decision. **Not authorized by it:** Gate I, Gate III, Gate IV or any gate; creation of the environment or its secret; any migration execution; credential provisioning or rotation (Tranche E); the Tranche D runtime cutover; blanket migration authority; and `D-1`, `D-2`, `D-3`, which remain separate. Gate IV's second prerequisite is met; Gate IV itself remains unissued. **Identifier:** allocated after the decision-rules §13 five-source search and **re-run at ratification** against `main` = `0e12e12143cb65fe877dfdad3d3b4c09602485a4` — zero collisions on both passes; `decision-id-reservations.md` v2.82 → v2.83 carries the row. **v4.51 (2026-09-02) — v4.50 clause 3 amended: CodeRabbit is withdrawn as an advisory reviewer, not retained; the Tier-2 round-1 FAIL on PR #326 resolved.** **Founder ruling, recorded verbatim:** "v4.50 clause 3 is amended to read: CodeRabbit is removed from the required-checks list on every MADVenturesLLC repository where it appears, and it is withdrawn as a reviewer of any kind; its GitHub App is uninstalled from every MADVentures repository where it was installed. Clauses 1, 2, and 4 of v4.50 stand unchanged. The advisory reviewers under clause 2 are Greptile, Copilot, the Claude Code review workflow authorized in v4.50, and Gemini Code Assist where installed." **Why:** v4.50 was written with clause 3 retaining CodeRabbit as an advisory reviewer; the Founder then uninstalled the CodeRabbit app from Build Room and removed the context from both rulesets, and the ops-actions-log rows of 2026-09-01T22:53:08Z and 2026-09-02T00:01:22Z recorded the acts against a ruling that said otherwise. Tier-2 round 1 on FounderOS PR #326 (reviewer gemini-3.1-pro, exact head 433819be8edbcb1ac46d65ed5f9b43b111c4f8d3) returned FAIL on that contradiction and ruled it a defect of the ruling or record for the Founder to resolve before merge. This entry resolves it on the ruling side: the executed state is the intended state, and the ruling is brought to it. This resolves the contradiction Tier-2 round 1 found; it does not clear Tier-2 itself — DEC-20260814-02's head-SHA binding still requires a fresh Tier-2 review and marker on whatever head this PR carries at merge time. **Executed state, by named check:** Build Room ruleset 20914346 required set `path-audit`, `attribution-shape`, `build-and-test` (rulesets API `updated_at` 2026-09-01T22:53:08Z); FounderOS ruleset 19355366 required set `attribution-shape`, `path-audit`, `tier2-shape` (rulesets API `updated_at` 2026-09-02T00:01:22Z). **App-uninstall state, Founder report only, not independently checked:** the CodeRabbit app uninstalled from Build Room by the Founder on 2026-09-01 (Founder clickwork, reported in session; no repo change); the CodeRabbit app uninstalled from FounderOS by the Founder between 2026-09-01T21:30Z and 2026-09-01T23:15Z (Founder's recollection: 5:30pm-7:15pm Eastern Daylight Time on 2026-09-01, reported in session; no repo change; precedes the ruleset edit at 2026-09-02T00:01:22Z the same evening Eastern). **Execution:** the ops-actions-log rows that recorded the divergence as open are closed by a builder commit on the same branch citing this entry; no further ruleset edits are called for. **Freeze posture:** same as v4.50, a narrow `DEC-20260814-03` clause-2 refinement expressly authorized by the Founder ruling above; it narrows the advisory-reviewer set and creates no new approval requirement, merge authority, or exception class; no clause-4 exception is claimed. **Not merge authorization:** same footer as v4.49. decision-log v4.50 → v4.51.



**v4.50 (2026-09-01) — required status checks ruled deterministic-only across MADVentures repositories; LLM review bots demoted to review-disposition inputs; advisory AI reviewers authorized.** **Founder ruling, recorded verbatim:** "1. Required status checks on protected branches are DETERMINISTIC gates only: path-audit, attribution-shape, build-and-test, and their per-repo equivalents. A check whose green can be emitted without the attested work occurring is ineligible to be required. 2. LLM review bots (CodeRabbit, Greptile, Copilot, and any added later) are review-disposition inputs under DEC-20260801-02 bar 2: their findings must be dispositioned on the record before authorization, but their status contexts gate nothing. 3. CodeRabbit is removed from the required-checks list on every MADVenturesLLC repository where it appears; it is retained as an advisory reviewer. 4. Tier-2 remains the independent review layer: roster reviewer, exact-head binding, closed verdict enum, model distinct from the implementation model. No inline bot substitutes for it." **Evidence, from the named checks of 2026-09-01 on Build Room PRs #11 and #12:** the `CodeRabbit` required context read `success` / "Review skipped: draft pull request" (attesting nothing); read `success` / "Review completed" at 16:03:58Z on a PR carrying **zero** CodeRabbit review objects (attesting a review that did not exist); and separately delivered a genuine Major finding with a self-verified fix confirmation and approval — establishing that the defect is the *gate semantics*, not the reviewer's finding quality, which is why clause 3 demotes rather than removes. **Execution:** ruleset edits are performed by the Founder directly per repository; Build Room's edit date and each subsequent repo's edit are recorded in the ops log as made. **Advisory reviewers authorized (automation, expressly authorized here rather than assumed):** installation of the Gemini Code Assist GitHub app across MADVentures repositories (Founder clickwork, no repo change); and a Claude Code review GitHub Action workflow per repository, landed by PR under each repo's own gates — noting `.github/workflows/` is a `DEC-20260814-02` Tier-2 trigger surface on FounderOS, so that repo's workflow PR takes the full chain. Neither advisory reviewer becomes a required context; both are bar-2 inputs under clause 2. **Freeze posture:** a narrow `DEC-20260814-03` clause-2 process refinement expressly authorized by the Founder ruling above, on the v4.45 pattern; it creates no new approval requirement, merge authority, or exception class, and no clause-4 exception is claimed. **Not merge authorization:** same footer as v4.49. decision-log v4.49 → v4.50.

**v4.49 (2026-09-01) — the seven Founder rulings and one determination shaping the Build Room command-journal contract; its ratification at Tier-2 round 5; its merge with passing post-merge verification; and the Phase 4 lane rebinding.** All recorded as an append to `DEC-20260827-01` (new Section 11, v0.10 → v0.11) — no new identifier is allocated and `decision-id-reservations.md` is untouched, matching the v4.47 pattern. The governed artifact is `docs/command-journal-contract.md` in `MADVenturesLLC/founder-os-build-room` — Build Room content, not carried by this repository; this entry records the Founder acts and the verification facts. **The rulings, in order, each posted on Build Room PR #11 and first recorded in the contract at the named revision.** (1) **§3 store and sole writer** (2026-09-01T01:41:57Z, v0.6): dedicated append-only, hash-chained event tables in the existing Build Room Neon Postgres; sole writer the control plane; append-only enforced by the database; §1.4's authority boundary against `build_room_events` affirmed as ruled text. (2) **§8.2 plan-decision authentication** (same comment, v0.6): the existing `PHASE3_ADJUDICATION_TOKEN` made a required configuration for the plan-decision endpoint, failing closed when unset; expressly Phase 4-scoped with Phase 5 the revisit point; **no new secret, no credential, provider, or spend authorized**. (3) **§12 terminal semantics** (05:42:44Z, v0.12): option (b) — Founder decision acts journaled as a distinct `decision` record class on the same chain; option (a) declined for four recorded reasons; the ruling covers T4, T5 and T22 as a class; five riding conditions including the record-class discriminator as an explicit field inside the canonical byte input for both classes, and §2's elements 4, 8 and 9 stated as not applicable rather than absent by omission. (4) **§5.3 authorization resolve-and-cover** (same comment, v0.12): reconciliation must resolve `authorization_ref` to a valid, applicable authorization whose granted scope covers the observed provider, model and execution surface; coverage may be by set or roster; silence is not coverage; a citation alone never reaches `completed`. (5) **`plan_hash` conditionality** (06:21:57Z, v0.14): required where a plan exists at the decision, absent where none does, the condition carried as a positive verifiable fact; no sentinel; no pre-plan sub-class. (6) **Coverage on every completion path** (06:59:40Z, v0.15): the resolve-and-cover test moved onto `completed` itself — the direct equal-route path is not exempt; reconciliation inherits the test by reference; one statement, no second copy. The same comment declined, on quoted evidence, a reviewer's stale re-file of the finding ruling 5 had already resolved. (7) **Plan-bearing state derivation** (07:17:58Z, v0.16): the ruled absent-set enumeration is replaced by a derivation — `RECONCILING` resolves to its stored `prior_state`; a printed set is accompanied by its derivation, and the derivation governs where they disagree; implemented with a reported full walk of the ratified table's only two unbounded transitions (T19, T22). **The determination** (06:47:17Z): the T4-return `PLANNING` case stays on the absent side, on **state-decidability** grounds — the recorded state alone must decide `plan_hash` applicability; no revision followed. A Founder-found claim-versus-requirement correction landed as v0.17: "T19 stores `prior_state`" described an unbuilt layer and was restated as a fail-closed obligation on the writer. **Defect class, named because it recurred on both sides of the table.** Rulings 3–7 are one failure in successive costumes: a stated condition narrower than the claim it supports — a citation standing in for a scope check, a vocabulary asserted closed with no legal path through it, an element set generalized from one transition, a hand-authored enumeration over an unwalked table, an assertion about unbuilt code. Three of those five narrow scopes originated in the Founder-side ruling drafting, not in builder implementation; the remedy adopted at ruling 7 — state the derivation, walk the class, never hand-author an enumeration — is the operative correction, and `RES-20260901-01` (draft) carries the full retrospective. **Authorization voided and preserved.** The Founder merge authorization of 04:38:24Z naming `283fccd7882bd77ddba72a05f569c52325ac3f49` was voided under `DEC-20260801-02` by a CodeRabbit `CHANGES_REQUESTED` at 04:52:39Z carrying three Majors against that head; the voided comment stands unedited as the audit record. Its single advisory (changelog lines quoting the §5.3 test) was dispositioned at 11:52:22Z: accepted as correct, declined as blocking, the non-normative-changelog header deferred to the contract's next revision — where the contract file's own `Status: proposed` frontmatter, stale against its ratified state, is corrected in the same pass. Rounds 1–4's verdicts bound earlier heads and are history. PR #11 merged 2026-09-01T12:06:15Z at merge commit `6ff0ee2893a22f59700f21ed825e8b974c3c3b9f` (the authorized head is its second parent, exactly); post-merge verification passed: 154-line merge-commit body, all six trailers present and unwrapped (longest 56 characters), `attribution-shape-check.sh main 6ff0ee2` PASS, push-gate green on `main` at the merge SHA, and the contract at `main` byte-identical to the reviewed v0.17 (sha256 `eaeb61789dcb40090e2384c04405f2b1a6b475c071bbf4e0cb50787cd5193e52`). **Lane rebinding.** At 12:23:46Z, under the Phase 4 authorization's own rebinding clause (`DEC-20260815-17`: "unless I separately authorize rebinding to a later base"), the Founder rebound the Phase 4 build lane to Build Room `main` at `6ff0ee2893a22f59700f21ed825e8b974c3c3b9f`, because step 2 must build on the ratified contract, which exists at no earlier base. It does **not** retroactively make the 19:51:06Z merge compliant with the pre-merge authorization requirement, and the record must not claim otherwise."* The Founder independently reviewed the exact PR head and the resulting merge commit before ratifying; no revert is required and none is performed. **Post-merge verification, run against the merge SHA per `DEC-20260721-01`:** merge-commit body 138 non-empty lines; Role-Id, Actor-Id, and Execution-Surface trailers present and unwrapped; `attribution-shape-check.sh main 8314c0a` PASS; push-gate Attribution Shape Check green on `main` at that SHA; merged head confirmed `311601b` by ancestry. **Authority boundary, restated from the disposition:** this corrective ruling grants no new product or execution authority. **v4.47 (2026-08-31) — Phase 4 (planner loop) authorized and the command-journal `DEFER` discharged at its named gate; both recorded as appends to existing ratified decisions.** Two Founder acts of 2026-08-31, issued after Phase 3 became durably closed at `6f5f4405da40c90028df0c1efefed2f44da65b1c` (PR #320, SHA-named merge authorization at head `63d7da008899e883f7bbbe9dde187c1ae27fedf1`). **(a) Command-journal ruling**, recorded in `DEC-20260827-01` (new Section 10, v0.10): the §3.2 `DEFER` is discharged at the Phase 4 gate it expressly reserved — not retroactively changed to `CONSUME` or `DIVERGE`, and §3.2's text is preserved byte-identical — and **Build Room shall establish one canonical governed command journal under Build Room authority**: exactly one canonical governed command history; append-only and tamper-evident; eleven required record elements; no credential material persisted; evidence-store custody and sole-writer authority unchanged; fail-closed — no governed command dispatches through the Phase 4 planner loop without its pre-dispatch journal record, and unresolved outcomes are never represented as success. The journal may reuse the §§3.3/3.5 consumed primitives where their frozen contracts permit; reuse does not make the evidence ledger the journal. Freeze disposition carried as written in the decision: not a governance change; clause 2's "not demanded by a shipping build" limb does not reach a journal demanded by the Build Room v1 slice (clause 7's own exit criterion), and the ruling executes `DEC-20260827-01` §3.2's pre-existing ratified obligation at its named gate; no clause-4 exception claimed. **(b) Phase 4 Founder Authorization**, recorded in `DEC-20260815-17` (new `## Founder Authorization — Phase 4 (Planner Loop) (2026-08-31)` section, v0.31), matching every prior phase: build lane opened from Build Room `main` at `ad23c6ea6117a54bce5be7208a5a5768ec5bfbc9` (isolated branch; rebinding to a later base requires separate authorization); journal-first implementation order — no governed Planner command executes before the journal passes its required tests; the four `DEC-20260827-01` CONSUME rulings (3.1, 3.3, 3.4, 3.5) implementable for Phase 4 under the frozen-consumption contract at MadBridge pin `7d37a61dcaeaef77c55013c7620deb8445726788`, drift-stop on material change; the Founder plan decision originates at the control plane on the `DEC-20260818-01` clause 1 pattern (neither the CLI — `DEC-20260815-13` clause 2 — nor the excluded web tier may originate it; any other surface requires a separate Founder ruling); rejection bound to the ratified `DEC-20260815-11` vocabulary (T4 `plan.revision_requested` or T22 `founder.cancel`, no third path, any new event or terminal state requires a lifecycle amendment ruled first); T5 approval enters BUILDING and dispatches nothing; a thirteen-item test and stop gate including exact-head Tier-2 review by the `DEC-20260814-02` ratified roster with the closed verdict enum and `DEC-20260815-05`'s model-distinctness constraint, and three consecutive governed runs under `DEC-20260815-17` clause 3 as affirmed, sourced, and scoped by the 2026-08-17 Founder Ruling (the gate's authority; `Fable BR-MVP-05` does not exist), with the counted-run entry authorization to define the Phase 4 run unit and its per-run-vs-set granularity. Freeze posture: clause-3 "venture and build work itself," stated rather than assumed within Section 2, matching Phase 2 and Phase 3.
