**REQUIRED STATUS CHECKS — `CodeRabbit` REMOVED AND RESTORED THE SAME DAY ON FounderOS `main` (2026-08-16); A SEPARATELY REPORTED GAP ON `founder-os-telegram`, NARROWED BY DIRECT WORKFLOW VERIFICATION TO A SETTINGS QUESTION.** **The round trip — all of it on 2026-08-16, in both UTC and Founder-local time.** The `CodeRabbit` required status check was removed from FounderOS `main` between **06:40:45Z** (`main`'s tip `20d2eca`, the last merge before it) and **06:51:03Z** (the first commit of the since-closed PR #260), and the Founder restored it at **13:48:18Z** the same day. **The restoration is independently verified, though not by `builder`.** The Tier-2 reviewer (`grok-4.5`, PR #261, head `04e2cf0`) performed a live ruleset read and reported ruleset **`19355366`** requiring `attribution-shape`, `path-audit` and `CodeRabbit`, the last pinned to integration **`347564`**; the restore itself is ruleset **history version `46679102`**, carrying the 13:48:18Z timestamp above. An earlier revision called `46679102` a *ruleset id*; it is a **version id** within ruleset `19355366`, and the mislabel was corrected on Tier-2 round 2. `builder` has no read path to branch protection and did not and could not confirm this itself — **the verification is the reviewer's, recorded as theirs.** An earlier revision of this entry gave the restore as “approximately 13:55Z” and labelled it `[Founder-reported]`; the live read supersedes both. **The restored `CodeRabbit` requirement is pinned to the publishing app rather than “Any source”, which is stricter than the configuration that was removed** and closes the defect recorded below — a context string alone can no longer satisfy it. An earlier draft of this entry dated these events *“2026-08-15/16”* and described the ruling as 2026-08-15 Founder-local: **both were wrong.** Founder-local is UTC−04:00, so 06:45Z is 02:45 local on 2026-08-16 — the same calendar date, not the day before. The ambiguity was flagged by the Tier-1 reviewer, and checking it showed the underlying date claim was also wrong, not merely imprecisely written. The removal followed a `builder` finding that was **false**: it reported `CodeRabbit` as undocumented drift *“added after 2026-07-27 with no decision record”*. In fact the Founder ordered it added **on 2026-07-27** — *“ratify 25-07 + Rule 1a: configure”* — as tabulated in `DEC-20260725-07`'s *Rule 1a configuration (2026-07-27) — implemented* section (ruleset `19355366`), and **`DEC-20260728-02` retired Rule 1a itself** — `01-constitution/operational-discipline.md` titles that section *“Rule 1a — REMOVED”* — **while its clause 3 separately retained the required status checks**, itemizing *“**CodeRabbit** (Tier-1) status check on each repository where configured”*. The two facts are distinct and an earlier revision stated only the second, writing that the decision retired *“only Rule 1a's approving-review requirement”*; that mirrors clause 3's own phrasing but reads as though Rule 1a survived minus a clause. It did not. Corrected on Tier-1 review of PR #261. **The same finding proposed re-dating that removal to 2026-07-29. Declined, with the reason recorded.** That date is the UTC framing of a Founder-local 2026-07-28 event: Founder-local is **UTC−04:00**, so an evening act lands on the following UTC day. The decision's own identifier, `DEC-20260728-02`, encodes the Founder-local date, and re-dating the prose would put the text at odds with the identifier.  `builder` read `DEC-20260725-07` **clause 2** — the 2026-07-25 finding that the required set was exactly `attribution-shape` + `path-audit` — and stopped, without reading that same decision's Status section, where the Founder's order two days later changed what clause 2 had just verified. **A verified finding quoted in the present tense past the documented event that superseded it: the defect class `DEC-20260725-07` exists to prevent, reproduced while citing `DEC-20260725-07` as authority.** Found by the Tier-1 reviewer. **No merges occurred in the window.** `main`'s tip `20d2eca` landed 2026-08-16T06:40:45Z, before the removal, and `main` did not move again while the check was off. **Nothing is superseded and no `DEC-` is allocated**: the removal was rescinded the same day with no merges under the weakened gate, so there is no changed state for an instrument to govern. The draft `DEC-20260815-19` written for the removal was **closed unmerged with PR #260** rather than landed, since a decision record for a rescinded action is itself a stale-premise trap. **A third `builder` error, named here because the self-account was otherwise incomplete** (Tier-2 MINOR, `grok-4.5` @ `04e2cf0`): a Constitution amendment was drafted for `01-constitution/operational-discipline.md` and **abandoned as wrong on its face** — it partitioned the Tier-1 role by repository, when `CodeRabbit` is Tier-1 for **all** MAD Ventures OS repositories under `DEC-20260719-02` as a uniform review obligation. Only the branch-protection wiring is per-repository. Earlier revisions implied this rather than stating it. **No Constitution amendment is needed.** `01-constitution/operational-discipline.md`'s Rule 1a section states the CodeRabbit (Tier-1) check remains required; restoring it makes that sentence true as written. The configuration drifted from the Constitution for a few hours, not the reverse. **Tier-1 coverage never lapsed.** CodeRabbit is the Tier-1 reviewer for **all** MAD Ventures OS repositories under `DEC-20260719-02` — a uniform review obligation, not a per-repository setting. Only the branch-protection wiring changed, and only on FounderOS. **Separately: required status checks were reported NOT configured on `founder-os-telegram`** (Founder-reported, 2026-08-16). **The workflow side of that report has now been verified directly**, by attaching both sibling repositories read-only and reading their workflow definitions. **`founder-os-telegram` @ `9bc1995`:** `.github/workflows/attribution-shape.yml` defines job `attribution-shape`, triggering on `pull_request` [`opened`, `synchronize`, `reopened`, `edited`] **and** `push` to `main`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml` defines jobs `verify` and `db-schema-verify`, triggering on `pull_request` to `main` **and** `push` to `main`. **`founder-os-console` @ `1c1f52f`:** `attribution-shape.yml` defines job `attribution-shape` on the same triggers; `ci.yml` defines job `ci`. **No job carries a `name:` override in either repository**, so each check context string equals its job id exactly. **Conclusion: every check context `DEC-20260728-02` clause 3 names already exists and already runs on pull requests in both repositories.** Marking them required therefore cannot produce an unsatisfiable gate — the failure shape that would have locked `main` had the FounderOS `CodeRabbit` requirement been re-sourced to GitHub Actions. Two contexts exist but are **not** in clause 3's required list: telegram's `verify` and console's `ci`. Neither repository defines a `tier2-shape` workflow, consistent with `DEC-20260814-02` Stage A being FounderOS-only. **What remains unverified is the branch-protection configuration itself** — whether these contexts are actually marked required. Attaching a repository yields its code, not its settings, and `builder` has no read path to branch protection on any of the three. So the Founder's report is **narrowed, not resolved**: if checks appeared absent on telegram, it is a branch-protection configuration matter and **not** a missing or mis-triggered workflow. **Configuring them is safe; confirming they are configured remains a Founder act.** **A fourth `builder` error, and the reason this paragraph is scoped rather than absolute** (Tier-2 round 2 MAJOR, `grok-4.5` @ `050ed6d`). An earlier revision claimed the FounderOS required set matched clause 3 **exactly** and that the change involved **no residual configuration change**. Both were false when written: ruleset `19355366` also required **`tier2-shape`**, added at **2026-08-16T14:26:39Z** (history version `46680555`) — before that revision was authored at 14:50:37Z. `tier2-shape` is `DEC-20260814-02` **Stage A, advisory**, and its own workflow reserves promotion to the required list as **Stage B, a separate explicit Founder direction**. Clause 3 does not name it. **This is the same present-tense live-state error the entry above already records twice** — a claim about a control surface authored after the surface had changed, by an author with no read path to it. Third occurrence in one package. **Resolution:** the Founder reported removing `tier2-shape` from the ruleset on 2026-08-16 (Founder-local ~11:40, ≈15:40Z; **Founder-reported, not `builder`-verified, and after the last independent read**), and states that **Stage B activation is planned for later the same day**. When that activation occurs it is a governance-machinery change requiring its own record, and if performed on or after 2026-08-17 it falls inside the `DEC-20260814-03` freeze window and needs a clause-4 exception. **Per-repository required-check configuration, Founder-stated 2026-08-16 and — following the `tier2-shape` removal above — matching `DEC-20260728-02` clause 3:** FounderOS `attribution-shape` + `path-audit`; `founder-os-telegram` `attribution-shape` + `db-schema-verify`; `founder-os-console` `attribution-shape`; plus the `CodeRabbit` Tier-1 check on each. **`path-audit` is FounderOS-only** — verified by direct read that no `path-audit` workflow or job exists in either sibling repository, so requiring it there would produce a permanently unsatisfiable gate. Checks produced by a repository's own workflows are pinned to GitHub Actions; `CodeRabbit` is pinned to its publishing app, **not** to Actions, which would never produce that context. **Evidence ladder — per claim, by source, time and control, because a blanket ladder contradicted itself.** An earlier revision said `builder` verified no settings **and** that all per-repository configuration was Founder-reported-and-unchecked, while also crediting Tier-2 with live settings reads of all three repositories. The second and third cannot both hold. Corrected on Tier-1 review of PR #261. What each source actually established** **(a) `builder`:** workflow definitions only — read directly from the repositories. No branch-protection read path on any of the three; nothing about settings is `builder`-verified anywhere in this entry. **(b) Founder — reported, and each of these post-dates the last independent read, so none is independently confirmed:** the `tier2-shape` removal from ruleset `19355366` (≈15:40Z 2026-08-16); the migration of `founder-os-telegram` and `founder-os-console` to rulesets only; and consequently whether `enforce_admins` survived that migration. A planned **Stage B** activation of `tier2-shape` later the same day is likewise Founder-stated and **has not occurred** as this is written. **(c) Independent — Tier-2 (`grok-4.5`) live reads, by round and control:** round 1 at head `04e2cf0` read FounderOS ruleset `19355366` and confirmed the restore at version `46679102`, 13:48:18Z. Round 2 at head `050ed6d` re-read `19355366` — finding `attribution-shape`, `path-audit`, `CodeRabbit` (app `347564`) **and** `tier2-shape` (added 14:26:39Z, version `46680555`) — and additionally read live protection on `founder-os-telegram` (required set matching clause 3) and `founder-os-console` (ruleset `20911135`, plus classic requiring `ci`). **Those reads were accurate when taken and are superseded for anything changed after them**, which is everything under (b). **Console carried two protection mechanisms with different sets** at the Tier-2 round-2 read: ruleset `20911135` requiring `attribution-shape` + `CodeRabbit`, and **classic branch protection additionally requiring `ci`** — a superset of clause 3, not a clause-3 miss, but a second source of truth for one gate. **That is no longer the live state.** The Founder reports that later on 2026-08-16 classic branch protection was removed from **both** `founder-os-telegram` and `founder-os-console`, leaving **all three repositories on rulesets only** — *Founder-reported, and after the last independent read.* Two consequences follow and are recorded rather than left implicit: **console's `ci` requirement is gone**, since it existed only on the classic mechanism, which brings console's required set to `attribution-shape` + `CodeRabbit` and closer to clause 3 than before; and **the `enforce_admins: true` that `DEC-20260725-07` records on both siblings' classic protection does not carry over automatically** — a ruleset grants bypass by its own bypass list, so whether admin enforcement survived the migration is **unestablished and outstanding**. Recorded because editing one mechanism while believing the other changed is precisely how the configuration drifted from the records in the first place. **No doctrine changes from the `CodeRabbit` restore:** it returns that check to the ratified configuration rather than altering it, so no amendment and no `DEC-` are required **for the restore**. That statement is deliberately scoped to the restore and makes no claim about the complete live FounderOS required list, which `builder` cannot read. **Founder-confirmed 2026-08-16: all three repositories are configured as described above.** **Time frame used throughout this entry, stated because an unstated frame already caused one error here.** **Dates are Founder-local (UTC−04:00), which is authoritative**; UTC instants are retained only where ordering is load-bearing and are marked `Z`. Founder ruling, 2026-08-16. Under it a Founder evening act lands on the next UTC day, which is why `DEC-20260728-02` is dated 2026-07-28 here and appears as 2026-07-29 in UTC-framed sources. Every event in this entry falls on **2026-08-16 in both frames**, so no date here changes under the ruling. **Two further transient configuration changes on 2026-08-16, recorded because ruleset version history preserves them and an omission here would repeat the `tier2-shape` defect.** The Founder added `ci` to `founder-os-console`'s ruleset `20911135` and `verify` to `founder-os-telegram`'s ruleset `19355000` — both real CI jobs (typecheck, lint, build/format, tests) and **both outside `DEC-20260728-02` clause 3**, which names neither — then removed both the same day, stating an intent to re-add them after this record is reviewed. `ci` had previously been required on console through classic branch protection and lapsed when that mechanism was retired; `verify` had never been required on telegram. **Net effect at the time of writing: all three repositories match clause 3 and nothing exceeds it.** Founder-reported; the FounderOS set was independently confirmed by Tier-2 at ruleset version `46683078`, 15:37:39Z, which also confirmed the `tier2-shape` removal. A future re-add would exceed clause 3 and needs its own record; on or after 2026-08-17 it also falls inside the `DEC-20260814-03` freeze. **A third transient, on FounderOS itself, recorded on the same standard.** `ci` was required on FounderOS ruleset `19355366` while **no FounderOS workflow declares a job named or id'd `ci`** — its three workflows carry job ids `attribution-shape`, `path-audit` and `tier2-shape`. That made the context unsatisfiable and left every pull request against `main` waiting on a status that could never report; PR #262 sat `blocked` on it. Found by `builder` 2026-08-16 by reading the ruleset and the workflow job ids directly — the same method used for the siblings above. The Founder removed it the same day; `builder` re-read the ruleset at 2026-08-16T21:01:48Z and the required set is now exactly `attribution-shape`, `path-audit`, `CodeRabbit` — matching `DEC-20260728-02` clause 3 with nothing exceeding it. **Cross-repository state at 2026-08-16, with the verification split stated because it is uneven.** `builder`-verified by direct API read at 2026-08-16T21:10:50Z: **FounderOS** `attribution-shape`, `path-audit`, `CodeRabbit`; **Build Room** `path-audit`, `attribution-shape`, `CodeRabbit`. Neither carries a `pull_request` rule, so no required-approval count applies to either. **Founder-attested and NOT `builder`-verified:** `founder-os-telegram` (`CodeRabbit`, `db-schema-verify`, `attribution-shape`) and `founder-os-console` (`attribution-shape`, `CodeRabbit`) — those two repositories are **not attached to this session**, so the API returns an access error rather than data and `builder` has no read path to them at all. On that split, all four match `DEC-20260728-02` clause 3 with nothing exceeding it, and console's `ci` and telegram's `verify` are both absent as recorded. This is the identical failure shape as console's `ci` and telegram's `verify`, on a third repository. **A control-surface weakening, found by Tier-2 round 3 and since reported fixed — the most consequential finding of the day.** `DEC-20260725-07` records `enforce_admins: true` on both siblings' **classic** branch protection, meaning administrators could not bypass. That property does **not** port to a ruleset, and Tier-2 (`grok-4.5`, head `da9ce69`) established by live read that **`founder-os-console`'s ruleset `20911135` granted `OrganizationAdmin` `bypass_mode: always`** — present from that ruleset's creation (version `46681409`) through version `46684385`. **The migration therefore silently removed admin enforcement on console.** `founder-os-telegram` was **not** affected — no `OrganizationAdmin` bypass, only Integration `85455`. FounderOS retains `OrganizationAdmin` always-bypass, which is the **pre-existing residual** `DEC-20260725-07` already documents, not a new loss. The Founder reports clearing console's bypass list on 2026-08-16, removing **two** entries — `OrganizationAdmin` and a **Cloudflare** integration. Tier-2's read named only the `OrganizationAdmin` entry; the Cloudflare entry is **Founder-reported and was not independently observed**, so the bypass list's full prior composition is not established here. Both removals are **Founder-reported, post-date the independent read, and are unverified at the time of writing.** **Recorded prominently because the entry's own open item is what surfaced it**: `builder` flagged that `enforce_admins` would not carry over, could not check it, wrote it down as outstanding, and an independent reader then established that the exposure was real. That sequence is the intended function of the two-tier review, and the exposure existed for roughly the interval between the migration and the fix. **Independently verified by Tier-2 round 3, listed so the record carries checks rather than reports:** `tier2-shape` off FounderOS at version `46683078`, 15:37:39Z; classic branch protection returning **404 on all three repositories**, confirming the ruleset-only migration; FounderOS live required set exactly `attribution-shape`, `path-audit`, `CodeRabbit`; console's `ci` added at `46683465` and dropped at `46684385`; telegram's `verify` added at `46683649`. The Founder subsequently reports `verify` removed from telegram, which — like the bypass fix — post-dates the independent read and is unverified here. **Freeze posture:** `DEC-20260814-03`'s window opens 2026-08-17 and was not operative for any event above. decision-log v4.15 → v4.16.

**WF-04 STEP 3 SLICE 1 AUTHORIZED by the Founder 2026-08-16 — contracts/ledger core in `MADVenturesLLC/founder-os-build-room`.** The Founder authorized the builder to implement the lifecycle specified in `DEC-20260815-11` v0.10 as pure TypeScript packages (`packages/contracts/`, `packages/ledger/`) with eight acceptance criteria: (1) all 17 states, 29 events, T1–T22 transitions and G1–G22 guards represented; (2) exhaustive transition rejection; (3) four core invariants each with a failing test; (4) non-plain transition semantics correct; (5) zero I/O verified by test; (6) every accepted transition records role, **actual model**, execution surface, scope, evidence, and resulting state; (7) attribution-shape and path-audit gates green in the Build Room repo; (8) prorated-daily vs book-full-month choice **surfaced and recorded** before merge — **not silently chosen**. **Two criteria were paraphrased in a way that dropped load-bearing words, and are restored above to the Founder's wording.** Criterion 6 read *"role, model, surface"*: the field is **actual model** — the model that in fact performed the work, which architecture §3.8 distinguishes from a nominal or configured one — and **execution surface**, a `surface_id`, not a bare "surface". Criterion 8 read *"surfaced before merge"*, dropping **"and recorded"** and **"not silently chosen"**; as paraphrased it could be satisfied by a remark in conversation, which is the opposite of what the criterion requires. **Repository `MADVenturesLLC/founder-os-build-room` created by the Founder 2026-08-15 under `DEC-20260815-01`** — private, with CI gates (attribution-shape, path-audit) adopted per clause 4 and the Fable planning package committed at `source/` as product input. **The creation date is 2026-08-15; an earlier revision of this entry gave 2026-08-16 and was wrong.** The repository's root commit is `c8bb063` — *"initial repository creation under DEC-20260815-01"* — authored **2026-08-15T21:36:05Z**, which is 17:36 on **2026-08-15** in Founder-local time (UTC−04:00), so the date holds in both frames and agrees with `DEC-20260815-01`'s own identifier date. Named check: `git log --reverse` over `origin/main` of the Build Room repository from a full (unshallowed) clone, run by `builder` at **2026-08-16T18:31:38Z**. **Only `858dd7a`, the CI-gates commit, is from 2026-08-16**; an earlier revision cited it as `main`'s tip *inside the creation clause*, conflating a later commit with the creation act and pinning a moving value into a fixed record. **Repository settings — secret scanning and push protection — are recorded on the Founder's own attestation and are NOT independently verified here.** `builder` has no read path to repository settings on this surface and did not and could not confirm them. Unlike the `CodeRabbit` ruleset verification recorded above, this attestation carries no named check, no timestamp and no independent read; it is stated as the Founder's, not as established. **Boundary:** zero I/O, zero credentials, zero infrastructure provisioning, no deployment, no provider access, no spend beyond what `DEC-20260815-09` and `DEC-20260815-16` already authorize for the build work itself. This is a `DEC-20260814-03` clause-3 record-keeping entry: the repository creation and authorization were separate Founder acts already performed; this entry records them. decision-log v4.16 → v4.17.

**`build-and-test` ADDED AS A THIRD REQUIRED CHECK ON THE BUILD ROOM (Founder, 2026-08-16) — `DEC-20260815-18` amended to v0.4.** `DEC-20260815-18` clause 1 named two required checks for `MADVenturesLLC/founder-os-build-room`: `attribution-shape` and `path-audit`. The Founder added a third on 2026-08-16 — *"I approve adding build-and-test as a required check"*, recorded verbatim in that decision's new `## Founder Addition` section. **That is the DECISION's approved set — `attribution-shape`, `path-audit`, `build-and-test` — and it is NOT a description of live settings; the two do not currently agree.** `build-and-test` is approved but **not yet applied**: `ci.yml` reached the Build Room's `main` only with the Slice 1 merge (`4155761`), and the requirement is added to the ruleset only afterwards. **`CodeRabbit` is required in settings and is not named in the decision's list** — live ruleset `20914346` (`builder` read 2026-08-16T20:52:39Z) requires `attribution-shape`, `path-audit`, **`CodeRabbit`** (publishing app `coderabbitai`, integration `347564`; **the required context string is `CodeRabbit` and `coderabbitai` is the app that publishes it** — two different fields, and an earlier revision of this entry used the app slug as the context. Tier-2 (`grok-4.5`) also reported a rename in ruleset history; **that is the reviewer's read and `builder` cannot confirm it**, since ruleset history returns 403 on this surface). Whether that obligation should be named in the decision's operative list is a **Founder determination and is left open**; `builder` does not write a check into a Founder decision's operative list. Raised by Tier-2 (`grok-4.5`, PR #262). **The addition is a new top-level section, not a fourth clause**: `## Decision` and `## Founder Authorization (2026-08-15)` stay **byte-identical to `c83a98f`** — the `main` tip this change was cut from, pinned rather than bare `main` so the baseline does not move on merge (Tier-2 `grok-4.5` MINOR) — following the structural correction Tier-2 forced on `DEC-20260815-11` at v0.5. Verified by `builder` 2026-08-16 by section-scoped hash against that commit. **A draft of this entry pinned that claim to `main@85b8179` and was wrong**: `85b8179` is PR #250, the *proposed* docket, where this file carries no `## Founder Authorization` section at all and `## Decision` opens "Upon Founder ratification, and not before:". The ratification merge `f79dc5a` is not the baseline either — `## Founder Authorization` has been stable since it, but `## Decision`'s preamble was reconciled afterwards, so only the current `main` tip — `c83a98f` — satisfies both sections. Caught by independent review before commit. **The context string is `build-and-test`, not `ci`** — the workflow is named CI but no job carries a `name:` override, so each context equals its job id; requiring `ci` would be a permanently unsatisfiable gate. Verified by `builder` reading the workflow definitions directly, 2026-08-16 — the same method used for the sibling repositories above. **Sequencing:** `ci.yml` is not on the Build Room's `main` at the time of this entry — it is introduced by the Slice 1 PR — so the requirement is added to the ruleset only after that PR merges; requiring it sooner would block any other PR branched from the current `main`, whose head carries no such workflow. Named check: `git cat-file -e origin/main:.github/workflows/ci.yml` over a full clone, run by `builder` 2026-08-16, which returned absent. **Recorded rather than left in settings alone** because a required check present in a ruleset and absent from its decision's operative list is exactly what was reported as undocumented drift on FounderOS earlier the same day, on a false finding, costing the `CodeRabbit` check a removal and same-day restoration. **The bypass list is NOT readable by `builder`, and an earlier revision of this entry wrongly reported it as empty.** The `bypass_actors` key is **absent from the API response entirely** on this surface — established by falsification 2026-08-16T20:52:39Z, since the same request against FounderOS ruleset `19355366`, recorded by `DEC-20260725-07` as retaining `OrganizationAdmin` always-bypass, also returns no such key. It was **absence of data reported as data**, then compounded by calling it an independent upgrade of the Founder's attestation. **On the only read that could see it, the list is not empty:** Tier-2 (`grok-4.5`) reported version `46692398` at 2026-08-16T19:32:30.548Z carrying `OrganizationAdmin` `bypass_mode: always`, added at `46690517` about four minutes after creation — **the reviewer's read, recorded as theirs.** **`OrganizationAdmin` is the Founder's own admin access — Founder-attested 2026-08-16 — not an unknown or third-party actor**, and `founder-os-telegram` additionally carries a Cloudflare integration alongside the same admin identity. An earlier revision of this entry called it an unresolved exposure by analogy to `founder-os-console`; that analogy was `builder`'s, drawn without knowing the principal, and is withdrawn. Recorded factually and without recommendation: `DEC-20260725-07`'s `enforce_admins: true` on classic protection does not port to a ruleset, so the posture differs — whether it should is a Founder determination, not a defect. **Freeze posture:** the addition was made 2026-08-16, before `DEC-20260814-03`'s window opens on 2026-08-17, so no clause-4 exception is required and none is claimed. **Scope:** binds this repository only; the FounderOS, `founder-os-telegram` and `founder-os-console` required sets are untouched and remain governed by `DEC-20260728-02` clause 3, which this entry does not amend. decision-log v4.17 → v4.21.

**`dec-consistency-check.py` ADDED to `/00-system/scripts/` 2026-08-15 on Founder approval — manual-only, CI wiring unauthorized.** `DEC-20260718-01` clause 1 authorizes that folder for static maintenance tools owned by `builder` **with founder approval for composition changes**; that approval was given 2026-08-15 (*"land the checker package"*). **No new DEC identifier allocated** — clause 1 requires founder approval for composition, not a new decision, and decision-rules §1 excludes it from requiring its own entry. **Stated precisely, because an earlier wording called it only "low-risk tooling maintenance" and that is incomplete (Tier-2 round 1):** this is **a composition change under `DEC-20260718-01` clause 1, DEC-optional under decision-rules §1, with CI wiring still forbidden.** The narrow reading is the correct authorization frame — a script added to an already-authorized folder under composition approval. The broad reading is the correct risk frame — a new check operators may come to lean on. Both are true and the record should carry both. **What it is:** a self-referential consistency check for `DEC-` files, catching the class where a claim true at revision N is falsified at N+1 and left standing. Seven rules: version/Notes coverage, stale landing-version claims, ruling counts, **a fixed set of "nothing remains open" phrasings** coexisting with an `## Open Item`, transition triggers resolving to the event enum under **documented** normalizations, delimiter claims matching trigger cells, and round-span counts. **Why it exists:** PR #257 drew **six Tier-2 rounds**, and **five of them found something** — six defects, **three of them introduced by `builder` while remediating the previous one**; round 6 (`gemini-3.1-pro`) returned an approving verdict with no finding, which is the round that carried the merge. An earlier wording of this entry said "six rounds and every round found something", contradicting `scripts/README.md` and `DEC-20260815-11`'s own Notes (*"revisions cut on this branch across five Tier-2 rounds"*) — caught by Tier-1 review, resolved against the merged record rather than by picking a number. The root cause was mechanical rather than attentional: these documents are hard-wrapped and every hand-run check was a **line-oriented grep**, so any claim spanning a line break was structurally invisible. A regex written for the exact round-3 defect returned zero matches because the sentence wraps. Every rule here matches whitespace-normalized text for that reason. **Validation:** each rule was written against a real defect from #257 and confirmed to fire on it before the fix was applied. `selftest` pins **30 fixtures, 17 mutation controls and a coverage check — 48 assertions, every rule reached**, including a **false positive this tool produced against already-merged correct text** — an earlier draft of the round-count rule flagged *"the same defect class two Tier-2 rounds already caught"* in `DEC-20260815-09`, which is a claim about how many rounds caught something, not a total. The rule is now restricted to span constructions and that sentence is pinned as a regression fixture. 10 fixtures pin a rule that must **not** fire, and four each build their own two-commit git repository, because rule 1 reads history and no text fixture can reach it. **Tier-2 round 3 (`chatgpt-5.6-terra`, REQUEST-CHANGES at `335bf03`) returned four MAJORs and a miscount, all reproduced and all taken.** Two were **silent misses** in the quote layer: a span crossing a `*` list item, and a span crossing from prose into a blockquote, each erasing an operative claim while reporting clean. Both existed because the previous round's calibration had dropped `*`/`+` as bullets and ignored `>` entirely **so the corpus would go quiet** — the reviewer's phrase, *"corpus quietness cannot substitute for preserving Markdown block context"*, is the correct principle and it reverses that call. Block crossing is now decided by comparing block **context** rather than scanning for marker characters, so a `>` continuation is the same block while a prose→blockquote transition is a crossing. **The honest consequence: `DEC-20260815-09` now reports ambiguous**, because its `+ ` continuation line is a list item per CommonMark. That finding is reported rather than tuned away. Third: **fenced code was masked for delimiter pairing but not for rule evidence**, so a fenced example containing `## Founder Ruling` inflated rule 3's count — a **tenth** judging path, and one where code data licensed a result. Fourth: the mutation harness caught `Exception`, which does not include `SystemExit`, so a mutant calling `sys.exit(0)` escaped looking green — the **third** instance of absence-as-evidence in that harness, after the crash case and the unevaluated-target case. And a miscount: both records claimed 12 negative fixtures where the suite has 10; the count had been produced by grepping output lines, which matched mutation labels too, and is now measured from the suite. **Tier-2 round 2 (`chatgpt-5.6-terra`, REQUEST-CHANGES at `b55b7cb`) returned two MAJORs and both were the same root, found two levels deeper than any previous round.** First: **parity is not a pairing test.** Two stray quotes give an EVEN count and still cross, so a four-quote document erased a real rule-3 defect while the guard reported "balanced" — the fail-closed guard added the round before was itself insufficient. Second: **the line-anchored extractors never went through the quote layer at all.** The `## Notes` section, `## Founder Ruling` sections, and the transition and enum tables all read raw text, so a quoted stale version satisfied rule 1 and a quoted obsolete ruling inflated rule 3's count. **That is sites eight and nine**, and the pattern across all of them is that each restatement of the invariant was too narrow to catch the next: *"rules read `unquoted`"* missed `addressed`; *"nothing reads `flat`"* missed extractors that read `text` rather than `flat`. A quotation is now defined structurally — inside one block, every delimiter consumed, code masked first — and every judging or licensing path reads one quote-safe view with layout preserved. **The first version of that structural rule flagged 15 of 116 correct DEC files**, because it treated `>` as a block boundary when verbatim Founder statements are routinely multi-line blockquotes, and read `**bold**` at a wrapped line start as a bullet. Calibrated against the corpus to zero ambiguous files across all 400 markdown files; the residual cost — `* ` and `+ ` not counted as bullets — is stated in the code rather than hidden. **The quote-exclusion defect was ultimately found at SIX separate sites across five rules, by three different reviewers, over four rounds** — rule 2 (Tier-1), rules 3 and 7 (Tier-2 round 1), rule 6 and rule 5's normalization detector (Tier-1 round 4, the last found by `builder` while fixing the one before it). **Fixing it by hand a sixth time was plainly not the remedy.** The invariant is now structural — **no rule reads `flat`**; quoted spans are stripped once into `unquoted` and every rule reads that — and each site is held by a mutation control. **Making every rule depend on that one layer then made the layer itself the exposure, and Tier-1 round 5 found it:** stripping pairs quote characters positionally, so a single **unmatched** quote makes every later pair straddle the wrong text and an operative claim between them is **deleted**. Reproduced on a genuine rule-3 defect that the checker reported **clean**. That is the worst outcome this tool can produce — a silent miss in the one layer all seven rules read — and it is not fixed by better pairing but by **failing closed**: when delimiters are unbalanced the tool does not strip at all and says so, accepting a visible false positive rather than an invisible false negative. No file in the corpus is currently unbalanced (0 of 116), so the defect was latent, not live. **Tier-1 round 6 then found the SEVENTH site, and it was not in a rule at all:** `addressed` — the set of normalizations rule 5 treats as documented — was read from raw text, so a normalization the document quoted as **withdrawn** still licensed the trigger it used to cover. Authority derived from the text, outside the rule bodies, and therefore outside the invariant as first stated. Everything that licenses or judges now goes through one `strip_quoted()` helper. **Rule 5's was the dangerous one:** it read `flat` when deciding whether a normalization was *documented*, so a normalization the document quoted as **withdrawn** still licensed the trigger it used to cover. That is a false **negative** — the checker silently certifying an unresolvable trigger — which for a tool whose purpose is to let review carry less is worse than any false positive. **Tier-2 round 1 (`grok-4.5`, REQUEST-CHANGES at `10e15bf`) returned four MAJOR findings, all reproduced and all taken.** Three were the same false-positive class in new places: rules **3 and 7** still scanned un-stripped text, so a document quoting a superseded ruling count or span claim was flagged — **the third and fourth instances of a defect Tier-1 had already found twice between rules 2 and 7**, which is why the fix is now enforced by mutation control rather than by remembering. Rule 7's unscoped `round N` collector read *"evaluation round 9"* as a Tier-2 round index. And **rule 4's documentation overstated it**: described as catching an operative "nothing remains open" claim, it matched one phrasing of three — *"Nothing remains open"* and *"No open items remain"* both passed silently. The regex now covers all three and every operator-facing description says it is a **phrase detector, not a semantic one**, because overstated coverage in a checker licenses skipping the manual check it exists to narrow. The fourth finding was the honesty one: **`README.md` claimed every git fixture "is checked against a deliberately broken copy of the rule it targets"** — a real step, done by hand, stated in the present tense as though the suite enforced it. It did not. **It does now:** ten mutation controls each break one rule, re-execute the mutated source, and assert the fixtures targeting it stop behaving correctly; deleting a pinned fixture fails the run. **The harness needed two corrections of its own, both caught by running it rather than reading it.** On its first execution every anchor matched twice — once in the rule, once as a literal in the mutation table — so it was substituting into the table; it now mutates only the region above the table and treats an ambiguous anchor as a failure, never a pass. And **Tier-1 round 4 found it had no mutation for rule 6 at all** while rule 1 had two, so it could report full health with rule 6 unguarded — which is exactly where the next defect was. A **coverage check** now asserts every rule 1–7 is named by at least one mutation. **Tier-1 round 5 then found two more in the harness itself, both taken:** a mutation that **crashed** was counted as *detected*, so a malformed replacement raising a `SyntaxError` passed the control without the fixtures ever running — the same empty result as the unmatched-anchor case, which was already treated as a failure; and the coverage check read the rule number out of the mutation's **label**, so a mutation could be *called* "rule 6" while breaking something else and listing another rule's fixtures and still pass. The rule id is now an explicit field checked against the targets. **Tier-1 round 6 then found a fourth:** a target fixture MISSING from the mutated run's results — git unavailable, or a fixture repository that failed to build — compared unequal to its baseline and was counted as **detected**, so both rule-1 controls could report success having executed nothing. The same class as the crash case, one line away from it. A target must now exist on both sides or the control fails. **Four corrections to the harness across four rounds** is the honest measure of it: it is useful, and it is not self-evidently correct. Three MINORs taken: rule 3's numbered-sub-step inflation (documented as a shape limit, since the rule cannot tell a ruling from a sub-step), rule 2's version token swallowing a sentence-ending period, and the parent `/00-system/scripts/` index row omitting the new tool. Two ADVISORY items taken as documentation: **exit 0 does not mean every rule ran** — rule 1 skips without git history and the exit code stays 0, so the status line has to be read — and the freeze position must be re-stated at merge time rather than assumed. Round 1 also confirmed the authority framing needed correction: *"low-risk tooling maintenance"* is incomplete, and the accurate statement is **a composition change under `DEC-20260718-01` clause 1, DEC-optional under decision-rules §1, with CI wiring still forbidden**. **Tier-1 round 2 found three more, all taken:** rule 1 collected version entries from the whole document, so a `**v0.4 (` mention in a ruling body or a quoted history could satisfy the requirement that the **Notes section** record that version — the inverse of the check; this file's Done Criteria said the path audit runs *"when practical"*, softening a control root `/CLAUDE.md` states as required; and this entry's own round count contradicted `scripts/README.md`. **Writing the fixture for the first of those exposed a defect in the test method itself:** all git scenarios shared one repository, so versions accumulated across them and the new fixture fired on a leftover rather than on the defect — it passed against the exact bug it was written to catch. Each scenario now builds its own repository, and each fixture was re-run **by hand** against a deliberately broken copy of its rule — a manual step at that point, which Tier-2 round 1 correctly refused to accept as a suite property; it is one now. **Tier-1 round 3 raised one finding, and it is the first one declined rather than taken:** it reported that the fixtures would fail on a host with no global git identity, having missed that every fixture git call already carries `-c user.email` / `-c user.name`. Reproduced with an empty `HOME` and `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL`/`GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM` at `/dev/null` — a bare `git commit` fails there with *"Author identity unknown"* while the script's form succeeds, and the suite ran clean (15 cases at that head; it has grown since). **The adjacent gap it pointed at was real and was taken:** the fixture setup did not check return codes, so a commit failing for any other reason would have produced a rule-1 finding derived from history never built — a broken fixture reading as a rule defect. Setup failures now return a SKIP that counts as a failure. **A sweep of all 116 `DEC-*.md` files flags zero** (no `DEC-*` file is modified by this branch, so that is `main`'s content). **The bound on that sweep, kept explicit:** because no `DEC-*` file differs from base, rule 1's version span is empty on every one of them — the sweep exercises rules 2–7 against the corpus and says nothing about rule 1 over each file's real history. Rule 1 is covered by the git fixtures instead. **That sweep also produced a second false positive, now fixed and pinned:** rule 1 flagged every version in `decision-log.md` and `system-index.md`, which have no `## Notes` section at all — the rule cannot judge a file that does not use the convention, and now reports that instead of flagging it. **And it produced one true finding in already-merged text it is not this change's place to edit:** the 4.14 entry below says the intermediate revisions were cut *"across four Tier-2 rounds"* while the same paragraph tallies **six**, and round 6's advisory drove a further revision. Left standing and disclosed here rather than corrected inside a tooling PR; the count is understated, not overstated. **The tool no longer detects it, and that is a cost of a later fix rather than a reason to stop disclosing it:** Tier-2 round 1 required rule 7's round collector to be scoped to the literal form *"Tier-2 round N"*, because an unscoped one read *"evaluation round 9"* as a round index and flagged correct text. The 4.14 entry writes *"round 6"* with no "Tier-2" adjacent, so the narrowed rule misses it — a deliberate false negative bought to remove a false-positive class, named in the script, the README and here rather than left for someone to rediscover. **Tier-1 review (CodeRabbit) returned six findings at `d18f203`, all verified valid and all taken.** Three were substantive: rule 2 scanned un-stripped text, so a document quoting a superseded version claim would be flagged — **the identical false-positive class already fixed in rule 7, left standing in another rule**; rule 5 skipped silently when a transition table had no event enum, which is the strongest form of the defect it exists to catch; and **rule 1 was never exercised at all** — every fixture ran with git disabled, so all 8 cases passed while rule 1 could have been entirely broken. It was not, but nothing in the suite established that. Three were minor: a claimed count of zero was swallowed as falsy by `or`-chaining, extra command-line arguments were ignored instead of returning the documented exit 2, and `scripts/README.md` restated a version that had gone stale — that last one is rule 2's own defect class in a non-`DEC` file, and the fix removes the restatement rather than bumping it. Each fix carries a fixture; breaking rule 1 deliberately was confirmed to fail the new git cases. **Limits, stated plainly:** it is shape-only in the same sense as the attribution checks — it detects internal contradiction, never whether a statement is true. **Two of #257's findings fall outside every rule and always will:** a completeness claim that was false, and governance authority asserted where none had been granted. Both required review. This narrows what review must carry; it does not replace it. **Boundary:** following `DEC-20260718-01` clause 6's model for `path-audit.sh`, **wiring this into CI or an unattended pre-commit hook is not authorized** by the composition approval and requires a further Founder decision. It creates no rule, gates nothing, and changes no audit surface. Freeze posture: the `DEC-20260814-03` window opens **2026-08-17** and is not operative on 2026-08-15, so no exception is claimed; were it operative, this is tooling that mechanizes an existing obligation rather than new governance machinery. **That argument is not free and must be re-stated at merge time rather than carried forward (Tier-2 round 1):** if merge lands on or after 2026-08-17 the window is open, and the "mechanizes an existing obligation" reading has to be argued against a package that does add a new operator-facing check to the OS surface. **Founder direction 2026-08-15, after three Tier-2 rounds: land it with the limits documented.** All three rounds returned REQUEST-CHANGES and every finding was reproduced and taken; the package is landing with its limits stated rather than closed, consolidated as a numbered **KNOWN LIMITS** block in `scripts/README.md` so an operator meets them before relying on a clean result. **No Tier-2 marker was attested on any round** — round 3's reviewer declined because its surface reports the model in loaded configuration but not from the runtime, and configuration is not independent proof of the responding model. `tier2-shape` therefore stays red on the merge head, which is a live gate question for the Founder rather than something this package resolves. `scripts/README.md` v0.4 → v0.5; system-index v1.14.26 → v1.14.27; decision-log v4.14 → v4.15.

**`DEC-20260815-09` MONTHLY SPEND CEILING SET and the `-09`/`-16` AGGREGATION RULED 2026-08-15 — USD 85 monthly, total spend, pausing above the ceiling; AND precedence with `-16`'s token ceilings.** Clause 1 of `DEC-20260815-09` reserved the monthly ceiling to the Founder. They set it on 2026-08-15 and then, in a second act the same day, ruled how it composes with `DEC-20260815-16`. Both rulings are recorded **verbatim** in `DEC-20260815-09` — `## Founder Ruling — Monthly Ceiling Set (2026-08-15)` and `## Founder Ruling — Aggregation Resolved (2026-08-15)`. That file is the single source; `DEC-20260815-16` cross-references it and carries no independent copy. **Ceiling:** value **USD 85**; scope **total**, infrastructure and provider API usage combined into one figure, superseding clause 1's parenthetical proposal that treated them separately (clause 1's own text unchanged); enforcement a **pause above the ceiling, not a hard stop** — spend **above** USD 85 pauses dispatch into a Founder-visible blocked state and resuming requires a Founder act to raise or cancel, adopting clause 2's wording (including its "above") unchanged. **The exact-boundary case is governed by ratified clause 2, not by the ruling:** an earlier revision read "at USD 85", which makes exactly USD 85 pause where clause 2 says only spend above the ceiling does. The Founder did not reach the equality case, so "at" was the drafter's word settling a boundary no ruling establishes — found in Tier-2 round 4 by `grok-4.5`, the same defect class rounds 1 and 2 caught. **Aggregation — all four questions closed by explicit Founder ruling.** (1) **AND precedence** — `-16`'s per-room token ceilings are inner guardrails, `-09`'s USD 85 the outer boundary; both must permit dispatch, either can pause. (2) **Infrastructure counted when incurred, not when invoiced.** (3) **Token→dollar via provider-reported tokens × the pinned `PRICE_TABLE_VERSION`, with the gate pausing on that estimate, not an invoice** — ruled knowingly against a figure `DEC-20260721-03` clause 3 labels non-authoritative, with `-16` clause 3's one-run overshoot allowance named by the Founder as *"the honesty mechanism"*. This **resolves rather than overrides** the round-1 Tier-2 objection: the defect there was an **agent** silently using a non-authoritative figure as an enforcement input; the Founder authorizing it knowingly and on the record is a different act. (4) **Calendar month, reset on the 1st.** Ceiling changes remain a single recorded Founder act. **One implementation choice is deliberately left open** — prorated-daily versus book-full-month recognition, which the Founder offered as two acceptable methods without selecting between them; that selection belongs to WF-04 Step 3 and is not made here, because making it would repeat the exact defect two Tier-2 rounds caught on this decision. The prior `draft` `builder` proposal is superseded and not reproduced. **`-16`'s clause text is unchanged** — it gains a cross-referencing ruling section only. **Scope of this change:** `DEC-20260815-09` clause 1 already authorizes the recurring spend *class*, ratified 2026-08-15; this change neither repeats nor narrows that, it records the bound on it. **Setting a bound is not itself an act of spending** — this change engages no provider, provisions no infrastructure, and builds no meter. Those are statements about what this text authorizes, verifiable by reading it, not claims about live-system state, and no live-system claim is made in either direction. **No cost-meter or ceiling-enforcement implementation was found in this repository by a narrow check, and the claim is bounded to that check:** four identifiers (`PRICE_TABLE_VERSION`, `spend[_-]?ceiling`, `monthly[_-]?ceiling`, `cost[_-]?meter`), case-insensitive, across `*.ts`/`*.tsx`/`*.js`/`*.py`/`*.sh`, at `fcffd5c` on 2026-08-15T18:54:43Z — zero hits, with no TS/JS source in the repository at all. It does **not** establish that no implementation exists under other identifiers or in another language, and it inspected `FounderOS` only — no claim is made about `founder-os-console` or `founder-os-telegram`. Building it is WF-04 Step 3 work and **requires a separate authorization that has not been given**. **Correction history, retained — six Tier-2 rounds; every verdict upheld.** Round 1 (`chatgpt-5.6-sol`, REQUEST-CHANGES at `fcffd5c`): an earlier revision recorded the aggregation rule as ruled and the gap as closed when the Founder had ruled only the value, the scope and the pause semantic; the sub-limit relationship, conversion mechanism, conjunctive precedence and overshoot extension were **authored** and were presented under a `## Founder Ruling` heading — a false attribution under agent-rules §10, not a wording defect. The conversion point was then withdrawn outright rather than relabelled, on the reasoning that `DEC-20260721-03` clause 3 labels displayed cost non-authoritative and clause 4 keeps dollar amounts out of the append-only ledger. Round 2 (same reviewer, REQUEST-CHANGES at `4dae500`): the operative value then read "USD 85 **per calendar month**" where the Founder had said only "monthly" — calendar-versus-rolling produces different pause behaviour for identical spend, and neither statement nor either ratified `## Decision` section established it; a smaller instance of the same defect. **The Founder has since ruled all of it**, including the calendar-month reset and the estimate-based gate the round-1 correction had withdrawn, so the substance now matches what those revisions asserted prematurely. That does not retroactively excuse them: the defect was asserting the answer before it existed, and the order in which things were actually established is recorded here deliberately. **Full round tally, so the count is not understated:** this package drew **six Tier-2 rounds**. Rounds 1 and 2 (`chatgpt-5.6-sol`) and rounds 4 and 5 (`grok-4.5`) each returned REQUEST-CHANGES and each found a real defect; round 3 was sent but voided by a push before a verdict returned; round 6 (`grok-4.5`) returned an approving verdict at `580dd3f` with two advisories and no finding above ADVISORY. **Round 5 found that this package's own claim of "zero residual instances" was false** — the check had matched the exact strings already known to be wrong instead of enumerating every site of the concept, the same way the round-1 file-coverage claim failed. **Round 6's remaining advisory was that these rounds were being fixed in place without being narrated, and that the header above said "two" while the text named four.** Taking it is what produced this paragraph, and doing so voided round 6's own approving verdict — recorded here rather than left implicit. `DEC-20260815-09` v0.2 → v0.6; `DEC-20260815-16` v0.2 → v0.6 (**v0.3, v0.4 and v0.5 are deliberate gaps `main` never carried** — intermediate revisions cut on this branch across four Tier-2 rounds, disclosed on the same standard as the v1.14.25 and 4.12 gaps rather than left as silent jumps); every ratified clause and both `## Founder Authorization` blocks byte-identical to `9b0f2ac`; system-index v1.14.24 → v1.14.26 (**v1.14.25 is a deliberate gap that `main` never carried** — the 1.14.25.x point releases existed only on this branch, to avoid clashing with PR #255 while both were open; disclosed here on the same standard as the 4.12 gap rather than left as a silent jump). **Merge ordering:** PR #255 (`DEC-20260719-02` roster reconciliation) merged to `main` first and took decision-log **4.13**; that package disclosed both orders as valid. This entry therefore numbers **4.14** rather than the 4.12.x it reserved while both were open, so the log stays monotonic and no version is claimed twice. **4.12 is a permanent, deliberate gap** — no entry ever carried it.

**`DEC-20260719-02` AMENDED v1.0 → v1.1 2026-08-15 on Founder direction — Tier-2 full roster reconciled to the ratified five-model list.** Two founder-directed changes: **added** `gpt-5.6-terra` and `gpt-5.6-luna`, and **removed** `opus-4.8` and `gpt-5.6`. The full roster is now `gemini-3.1-pro`, `gpt-5.6-sol`, `gpt-5.6-terra`, `gpt-5.6-luna`, `grok-4.5`; the restricted local roster (`hermes-4-14b`, `tencent-hy3`) and clause 5's non-binding rule are unchanged, and clause 1's escape-hatch ban is retained. **Authority:** the additions rest on the Founder's 2026-08-14 ratification statement recorded verbatim in `DEC-20260814-03` (*"reviewer is gemini-3.1-pro, Codex models (Chatgpt 5.6 Sol, Terra and Luna) and Grok 4.5"*) — the same statement `DEC-20260814-02`'s gate roster was built from, and quoted in this log's 2026-08-14 ratification entry; the removals rest on the Founder's direction of 2026-08-15 (*"take opus-4.8 and gpt-5.6 off"*), delivered in the same session as explicit approval to edit `/01-constitution/agent-rules.md`. **What this fixes:** from 2026-08-14 the `tier2-shape` gate enforced the ratified five-model roster while `DEC-20260719-02`, `agent-rules.md` §3.6, `/04-agents/model-registry.md`, `/04-agents/role-registry.md`, `/04-agents/roles/independent-reviewer.md`, and `/06-prompts/independent-reviewer-tier-2.md` still carried the older one — the roster governing on paper and the roster governing in CI were different lists. The model registry additionally asserted *"This model cannot satisfy Tier-2"* for `gpt-5.6-terra` while the gate accepted it, and `chatgpt-5.6-luna` resolved to **no registered model at all**. Three of those files (`role-registry`, the role contract, and — for the roster line — the prompt) were still at v0.8 and had never picked up v0.9's `gpt-5.6-sol` either, so the drift predates the gate. **Correction (Tier-2 review, `gemini-3.1-pro`, REQUEST-CHANGES at `e8a5a5d`, 2026-08-15): the package first claimed six live files and was wrong — the roster reached eight.** Tier-2 found two the author's audit had missed: `/05-workflows/wf-13-code-review.md`, which governs the merge gate and both listed the stale roster *and* asserted "`gpt-5.6-terra` is not Tier-2" — the exact contradiction this package exists to remove, sitting in the workflow that decides merges — and `/06-prompts/prompt-index.md`. A subsequent exhaustive audit found three more the Tier-2 review had not flagged: the `gpt-5.5` entry's compatibility note in `model-registry.md` asserting `gpt-5.6` "remains the Tier-2 review SKU", and stale references in `DEC-20260812-02` and `DEC-20260730-04`. The first three are operative and are corrected; the two decision references receive dated supersession notes with **no ratified clause altered**, since `DEC-20260730-04`'s own text already requires the monitor to discover the roster dynamically from the registry rather than hard-code it. The original claim failed on the same axis it was fixing: a completeness assertion that had not been exhaustively verified. **Not retroactive:** Tier-2 verdicts issued by `opus-4.8` or `gpt-5.6` while bound remain valid for the work they reviewed; both entries are retained with `retired_role_bindings` and dated history, not deleted. **`model_id` vs attestation id recorded:** the `Tier2-Reviewer-Id:` token the gate accepts is the `chatgpt-` form for the three Codex models, while the registry — per `DEC-20260807-01` — spells them `gpt-`; a marker written with the `model_id` form fails the gate. Neither spelling is renamed (renaming would break `DEC-20260807-01`'s references or edit a ratified gate); the mapping is recorded in every roster location and flagged as an open item to collapse to one spelling. **`gpt-5.6-luna` created with `last_verified: verification-pending` and `source: verification-pending`** — no provider catalog check has been run for it and none is invented; the entry is a governance record, not a verification claim. **No new DEC identifier allocated** — clause 1 of the decision being amended requires "a new decision **or an amendment to this one**", and this is the amendment; decision-rules §1 excludes it from a separate entry. **Freeze position (`DEC-20260814-03`):** executed 2026-08-15, two days before the 2026-08-17 window opens, so no clause-4 exception is required; it would in any case be clause-3 record-keeping, creating no rule and recording a roster the Founder had already ratified and shipped into CI. `DEC-20260719-02` v1.0 → v1.1; `agent-rules.md` v1.0 → v1.1; `model-registry.md` v0.17 → v0.18; `role-registry.md` v0.20 → v0.21; `independent-reviewer.md` v0.5 → v0.6; `independent-reviewer-tier-2.md` v0.4 → v0.5; `model-portfolio-and-routing-strategy.md` v0.2 → v0.3; decision-log v4.11 → v4.13; `wf-13-code-review.md` v0.7 → v0.8; `prompt-index.md` v0.4 → v0.5; `DEC-20260812-02` v1.0 → v1.0.1; `DEC-20260730-04` v1.1 → v1.1.1. **On the version numbering:** 4.12 is claimed by PR #254 (the `-09`/`-16` spend ceiling), which was open and in **draft** state when this entry was written. This package numbers past it so the two cannot land a duplicate version. These numbers are **allocations, not a merge-order guarantee**, and neither package depends on the other — both orders are valid and neither invalidates this entry: (a) if #254 merges first the log reads 4.12 then 4.13, in sequence; (b) if this merges first the log carries 4.13 with 4.12 arriving afterwards, so the file's version briefly runs ahead of its newest-but-one entry. That is disclosed here rather than left silent, and it is recoverable — a later entry can renumber if the founder prefers strict monotonicity. (c) If #254 is abandoned, 4.12 is a permanent deliberate gap. A visible gap or a brief out-of-order step is preferable to two packages silently claiming one version number.

**WF-10 Run #2 completed 2026-08-14 — second live run of the Decision Review workflow.** Working set: 3 due `monthly` decisions (DEC-20260713-01, DEC-20260714-01, DEC-20260715-15), 0 renew-in-place, 0 open escalations, 0 red health. Founder ruled all three **correct**; all three review dates renewed +30 days to **2026-09-13**. DEC-20260714-01 is ruled correct **as the historical record it was frozen as** — `DEC-20260717-02` clause 3 preserves it byte-identically at `proposed` status, and `DEC-20260801-01` clause 9 already authorizes the deputy/zai/glm-5.2 runtime activation; this run does not reopen that settlement. **Ruling history (logged, not rewritten):** the founder's initial Step-3 word on DEC-20260714-01 was "Ratify", delivered on a Step 1 compilation that omitted those two successor decisions; Tier-2 review ruled the resulting promotion unreconciled (BLOCKING, F1, verdict REQUEST-CHANGES at head `ecd152d`), and the founder re-ruled Option B — correct, keep proposed. Audit samples: the one merge commit touching `07-decisions/` in the window (`af9fdf7`) carries valid `Role-Id: builder` (no clause-23 violation); zero DEC files amended directly on main; one direct-to-main commit `f41234a` editing `decision-id-reservations.md` without an ops-log row, routed to Step 7 and ruled no action. Run record: `/10-operating-cadence/decision-review-2026-08-14.md`. This is WF-10 Run #2 of 3 required before Phase 4 EWP eligibility. decision-log v4.9 → v4.10.

**`DEC-20260809-01` DECISION HEALTH RECONCILED `yellow` → `green` 2026-08-14 by explicit Founder ruling**, after PR [#244](https://github.com/MADVenturesLLC/FounderOS/pull/244) merged to `main` at `479e4bff61b7993c2d0763376188a4339f577c0b`. The ruling, recorded verbatim in that decision's `## Decision Health` section: *"I, Michael Daley, explicitly rule that the current Decision Health of `DEC-20260809-01-madventures-os-build-room-wf03-resumption.md` is now `green`."* **Basis:** that section made health conditional — "moves to `green` when the remaining prerequisite is closed" — and named the prerequisite as the absent WF-03 inputs `product-brief.md` and `roadmap.md`. Both were created and merged 2026-08-13 via PR #235, unblocking Steps 3 and 4; WF-03 Steps 3, 4 and 5 were then each separately Founder-authorized and completed (Steps 3–4 on 2026-08-13, Step 5 on 2026-08-14 under `DEC-20260814-04`), with Step 5 landing on `main` through #244. The second prerequisite closed by Founder ruling 2026-08-10. Per decision-rules §7, `green` = "clear, owned, current, and actionable." **The earlier `yellow` was correct when recorded and the Step 5 hold was not an error** — the condition simply came true; the original `yellow` assessment is preserved verbatim in that section, and the dated 2026-08-14 current-state note recording that `builder` flagged rather than re-rated the field is retained as written. **`green` authorizes nothing:** WF-04 remains unauthorized and unexecuted, PRD approval confers no implementation authority, and no infrastructure, credential, repository, runtime, deployment, activation, or binding authority is created or altered. **No new DEC identifier allocated** — decision-rules §7 treats health as an executability measure and §1 excludes low-risk maintenance from requiring a decision entry; `DEC-20260814-03` clause 3 exempts this class of record-keeping from the freeze. `DEC-20260809-01` v2.0 → v2.1; decision-log v4.8 → v4.9; system-index v1.14.21 → v1.14.22.

**WF-03 STEP 2 ANSWERED by the Founder 2026-08-10 — Steps 1 and 2 of WF-03's first live run are complete.** All ten `/01-constitution/product-philosophy.md` §6 questions were answered in the Founder's own words and are recorded **verbatim** in `/10-operating-cadence/wf-03-run-madventures-os-build-room.md` (v0.1 → v0.4), together with one Founder addendum to answer 10. Nothing was drafted, inferred, summarised, or normalised by `builder`. **The answers are product definition, not authority.** Answers 9 and 10 and the addendum state what the Build Room must do — Founder sign-in, provider connection flows, connection of active Founder-approved model-role assignments through provider-approved authorization methods, and a visible distinction between connected, expired, unavailable, suspended and unauthorized agents — with the role and execution-surface registries remaining the source of truth for eligibility and authority, and connection granting no new authority, no self-assignment, and no bypass of Founder approval. Answer 10 **defers** unsupported third-party OAuth work, cloud custody of provider credentials, autonomous execution, autonomous merge/deployment, agent marketplaces, and unrestricted collaboration, requires provider authorization to use each provider's approved method with credentials held locally by the Agent Gateway where required, and directs that other planned ventures are not built through this initiative. Answer 8 records the 30-day failure question as **"Unknown today"**, naming context growth, ambiguous handoff ownership, agent-routing mistakes, approval fatigue, and parallel-build visibility as the first likely pressure points, and requires the MVP to capture evidence on them before autonomy expands. **Every exclusion in `DEC-20260809-01`'s Scope section stands unchanged**, and **Steps 3, 4 and 5 have not run** — 3 and 4 stay blocked on the missing `mad-ventures-os` inputs, 5 is a separate later Founder act, and the Founder directed that Steps 3–5 not begin and that no additional product artifact be created. `DEC-20260809-01` v1.4 → v1.9 (Next Action items 1 and 2 both complete; product-directory references corrected; dated supersession note added below the preserved ratified Scope); WF-03 v0.4 → v0.5; system-index v1.14.12 → v1.14.13; workflow-registry v0.10 → v0.11. **Merged to `main` 2026-08-10 as `54449f4a99e6b804bd40bb290cd14608e0c0e9d8` (PR #227, authorized head `6f2fc6a1ee2fca329730cd137cfa0b0d219f7ce8`).** Post-merge verification per `DEC-20260721-01`: merge-commit body non-empty, all three Attribution trailers present, and `attribution-shape-check.sh main 54449f4` **PASS**. The merge used a caller-supplied `commit_title`/`commit_message`; that is recorded as a fact about this merge, **not** as a preferred technique — the founder ruled 2026-08-10 that no merge path is reliable and that post-merge verification is the control.

**WF-03 STEP 1 EXECUTED AND ACCEPTED by the Founder 2026-08-10 — WF-03's first live run.** MADVentures_OS Build Room is accepted into WF-03 product-definition work. Per the Conflict 1 ruling the outcome is recorded in the run record `/10-operating-cadence/wf-03-run-madventures-os-build-room.md`, **not** in `DEC-20260809-01`, which holds authorization and boundaries and would have held a reject/defer. The Founder's decision is recorded verbatim there, including the product definition: *"a governed multi-agent delivery environment that turns an approved Founder or FounderOS objective into a traceable planning, build, review, verification, and pull-request process."* **Four of `/01-constitution/product-philosophy.md` §4's nine criteria were named** — decision follow-through, drift prevention, accountability, time-to-action — which clears §4's disjunctive bar; the other five were not claimed and no claim is made for them. **The acceptance creates no new authority by its own terms:** it authorizes only WF-03 Step 2 and the governed product-definition path already established, and expressly not implementation, infrastructure, provider credentials, OAuth, repository creation, execution orchestration, deployment, or any expansion beyond `DEC-20260809-01`'s separate gates. **Step 2 is presented and awaiting Founder answers** *(superseded within the same day — the Founder answered all ten questions on 2026-08-10; see the entry above)*; Steps 3 and 4 stay blocked on the missing inputs `product-brief.md` and `roadmap.md`; Step 5 remains a separate later act. `DEC-20260809-01` v1.3 → v1.4 (Next Action item 1 complete, Contingency not triggered, on-change review trigger fired); WF-03 v0.3 → v0.4 — its `not yet run live` status is now **false** and has been corrected wherever it appeared. Decision Health stays `yellow` on the one open prerequisite.

**FOUNDER RULINGS 2026-08-10 — WF-03 Reconciliation Conflict RESOLVED (`DEC-20260809-01` v1.2 → v1.3, WF-03 v0.2 → v0.3).** The two conflicts recorded unresolved at ratification are ruled. **Conflict 1 — Step 2's destination:** on an accepted run the ten answered product-review questions are recorded in a WF-03 run record under `/10-operating-cadence/`, rather than in the PRD draft that Step 3 has not been authorized to create. The record is created when the run happens and its filename is fixed then; no governed file names it now, since a path that does not resolve must not be written into one. **Conflict 2 — reject/defer:** if Step 1 rejects or defers, Step 2 does not run, **no run record is created**, and that outcome is recorded in `DEC-20260809-01` itself rather than on a `roadmap.md` that does not exist for `mad-ventures-os`. Candidate 1 (define a pre-artifact record) was selected; candidate 2 (narrow the Next Action, deferring Step 2) was **not** taken. **Both rulings are scoped to this run only:** WF-03 v0.3 carries them as a run-scoped amendment naming the MADVentures_OS Build Room run and no other, with Step 2's general output line untouched; whether either becomes general doctrine is expressly left open for a separate Founder decision. Founder framing, recorded verbatim: *"This is a pre-execution specification conflict discovered while preparing WF-03's first live run. Scoping the resolution to this run lets FounderOS test the record shape before making it general doctrine."* **Separation of records as the Founder set it, conditional on the Step 1 outcome:** on an accept, run record = Step 1 acceptance + Step 2 answers; on a reject/defer, no run record exists and the decision carries the outcome. In both cases the decision holds authorization and boundaries; the PRD is created only when Step 3 is authorized; a handoff only when work passes to another accountable operator. **What the rulings do not do:** they authorize no WF-03 step to run and imply none has run — WF-03 stays `not yet run live`, and every Founder Ratification boundary is untouched. **Decision Health stays `yellow`, now on one open prerequisite rather than two:** the WF-03 inputs `product-brief.md` and `roadmap.md` still do not exist for `mad-ventures-os`, so Steps 3 and 4 remain unexecutable — the Conflict 2 ruling removes the *reject/defer* dependency on `roadmap.md` but not Step 4's draft-row dependency. Step 1's acceptance decision is now executable for all three of its outcomes, where before only an accept had a defined record location.

**FOUNDER RULING 2026-08-10 — "no merge path is reliable; verification is the control."** The bare-API merge — `merge_pull_request` with no caller-supplied `commit_title`/`commit_message`, the exact form adopted by the 2026-08-03 ruling — produced a **title-only merge commit with an empty body and no Attribution trailers** at `2f414ca` (FounderOS #222, merged 12:00:46Z). The push gate fails on it: `FAIL (c) final-merged-commit 2f414ca…: no valid Attribution block`. This falsifies the "reliable workaround" characterisation recorded on 2026-08-03, which is **withdrawn** here and in `DEC-20260721-01`; the 2026-08-03 adoption is retained as historical record, not as current practice. **The PR-side controls all held** — trailer block at column 0, body ending in a pure trailer block, both required checks green on the authorized head, no `edited` event between the final body check and the merge — so the failure is in what GitHub wrote as the merge-commit message, not in the PR. **Method choice was `builder`'s, not the founder's:** the `DEC-20260718-04` authorization named the head SHA and authorized the merge, not how to execute it, and `builder` chose the bare-API form *because* it was the approved one. **Judged on the criterion the gate applies — did the final merge commit carry a valid Attribution block — only the bare variant has ever failed**, once in three uses (`74f217e`, `25e60e5` valid; `2f414ca` invalid), while the explicit-message variant is two for two (`aa715a3`, `3979d94`). A separate and weaker inconsistency sits on *message source*: #130 reports the supplied message discarded in favour of the PR body, #220 landed it verbatim — but `aa715a3`'s Attribution block was valid regardless, so that is a message-source mismatch and not an Attribution failure. Two successes is not a reliability finding and contradictory message-source behaviour is not a recommendation, so **neither variant is adopted**; **cause unidentified and recorded as unknown** — three hypotheses have now been advanced and not held in `DEC-20260721-01` — the hand-cleared merge dialog (recorded as wrong below), a web-UI attribution for the empty-bodied merges (withdrawn as unsupported), and now **the GitHub mobile app**, raised by the founder on 2026-08-10 and tested against the client information they supplied: the app was used for #217, #219 **and #221**, and `edd2193` (#221) carried a valid 4,218-byte Attribution block while the other two landed empty, so the same client produced both outcomes and cannot alone account for the pattern — nor can it explain `2f414ca`, which `builder` merged through the API with no founder client involved. **Browser data point added the same day:** the founder merged the PR carrying that record from a desktop browser as a deliberate test — `1961397d636c8c7e09371f2957797312acae43b6`, 5,969-byte body, all three trailers, both gates PASS — which gives every **identified** client variant at least one observation, `558a64f` remaining unclassified because its client is not recalled. It is **one row, not a finding**: the mobile app was also one-for-one until `5e4dd44`, so a single compliant merge exonerates no client variant. It settles nothing causal either — an earlier draft claimed it showed browser-versus-not cannot explain `2f414ca`, which is backwards and withdrawn, since `2f414ca` is a non-browser merge that failed and is therefore consistent with such a split; what makes that split doubtful is the five non-browser merges that succeeded (`aa715a3`, `74f217e`, `25e60e5`, `3979d94`, `edd2193`), which was already true before the test. The merge dialog's pre-fill state was not observed, which remains the one measurement that would separate a pre-fill failure from something downstream. The repository setting's state at this merge was not independently checked, so no claim is made about it. Ruling: no merge technique is approved or preferred; **the merge commit is verified after every merge, whatever the path**; merge execution stays with `builder` on founder authorization. Following the `17a3c5e`/`1ac5bfe` and 2026-08-03 precedents, **no backfill** — `2f414ca` stays on `main` without an Attribution block, its substantive change unaffected, and `DEC-20260721-01` v1.12 → v1.13 carries the compensating record. Open: `main` now carries a third failing push run in two days, after `5e4dd44` and `e7f2e1d5`.
