**TWO FOUNDER RULINGS 2026-08-03 — Rule 3's two-flag conflation, and the trailer-less merge commits.** Both were raised by `builder` as questions the builder lane could not answer alone, and both were ruled in the founder's words.

**(1) Rule 3 conflation — "Constitution moves; Console stays."** `/01-constitution/operational-discipline.md` Rule 3's `LEDGER_BACKED` Note said flipping the constant "makes the Approval Queue's non-ledger disclosure stop rendering." It does not, and never did: `LEDGER_BACKED` governs the **Activity feed** disclosure, while the **Approval Queue** carries a separate `ledgerBacked` on `ApprovalQueueResult`, hardcoded `false` in `founder-os-console` `lib/approvals.ts` and `app/server/approvals/index.ts`. The 2026-08-02 activation did not move it, and that screen's disclosure **still renders, correctly**, because the queue reads from the Console's own Supabase rather than the ledger. The founder ruled the **constitution** is the side that moves; the Console is unchanged and must not be "fixed" to match the old wording — suppressing a true disclosure to satisfy a mistaken sentence is the `agent-rules.md` §10 honesty failure Rule 3 exists to prevent. **`builder`'s first correction was also wrong and was caught in independent review before merging:** it said `LEDGER_BACKED` governs the *Activity feed's* disclosure instead. Read directly from `founder-os-console` `main`, the constant renders **nothing** — `ActivityFeed.tsx` receives the *projection's* `ledgerBacked`, a literal `false` since PR #39 decoupled them, so the Activity disclosure renders too. **Both disclosures currently render, both correctly, and the 2026-08-02 activation was never capable of suppressing either.** Constitution v1.13 → v1.14 records both the original error and `builder`'s, rather than quietly replacing one with the other — a rule misstated twice in the same direction is worth reading carefully. Three flags, three meanings: one gate-state constant, one projection-provenance flag, one queue-provenance flag.

**(2) Trailer-less merge commits — "log and compensate; never rewrite published `main` history."** Three merge commits landed 2026-08-03 carrying only the PR title, therefore no Attribution trailers: `2835fe8` (FounderOS #197, 08:33:04), `63edab7` (`founder-os-console` #39, 09:47:12), `28dd8a1` (FounderOS #201, 09:49:56). **The push backstop did not miss them** — it ran on all three and failed on all three, leaving FounderOS `main` red on a required check from 12:33Z until `25e60e5` merged green at 17:10Z. The gate worked; the failure was upstream of it. **Cause unknown and recorded as unknown:** the founder verified the merge settings as correct while the failures occurred — across all three repositories, covering both of the two affected — ruling out settings drift — so this is *not* a recurrence of the 2026-07-26 cause despite the identical symptom. An early `builder` diagnosis blaming a hand-cleared merge dialog is **recorded as wrong**: `2835fe8` failed first, on a PR the founder had not touched that way. What is known is correlation only — the day's two API merges (`74f217e`, `25e60e5`) carried full bodies and passed, the three web merges failed — but `e09bb72` was a web merge on 2026-08-02 that passed, so the simple reading is already falsified. The API path is adopted as a workaround, not an explanation. *(**Superseded 2026-08-10** — the word was "reliable" here, and it is withdrawn: the bare-API merge produced a title-only commit at `2f414ca` (#222), and the founder ruled the same day that **no merge path is established as reliable** and that post-merge verification is the control. See the 2026-08-10 entry above and `DEC-20260721-01` v1.13.)* Following the `17a3c5e`/`1ac5bfe` precedent, **no backfill**: rewriting `main` would destroy published commit identities other records cite, `28dd8a1` in particular being named in this log as the commit recording the clause 2 ruling. `DEC-20260721-01` v1.8 → v1.9 carries the compensating record.

**`DEC-20260725-05` clause 2 (durable recording) RULED MET by the Founder 2026-08-03 — Phase 3 is COMPLETE.** Ruling issued in the working session, verbatim: *"I rule it was met for Clause 2."* Both acceptance clauses of `design-architecture.md` §23 Phase 3 are now met — clause 1 (decision-capture) on 2026-07-25, clause 2 on 2026-08-03 — and the record moves from PARTIAL to complete (`DEC-20260725-05` v0.2, Decision Health amber → green). **Clause 4's mandated phrase — "decision-capture complete, durability open (Neon sequence)" — is SUPERSEDED and must no longer be used**; it described the PARTIAL state, and repeating it is now the `DEC-20260725-01` clause 15 misstatement in the opposite direction from the one originally feared. Clause 2's basis had been three items: two are no longer true — the Neon evidence plane is operational (activated 2026-08-02, `founder-os-console` `main@e09bb72`) and Console approval decisions no longer live only in Supabase (`DEC-20260731-05`'s runtime-owned write path, condition 4 ruled met 2026-08-01) — and the third, that the Console has **no evidence-writing authority** (`DEC-20260717-03` items 6–7), remains true — that decision is unchanged as of this ruling — and was never a gap to close: it is *why* the runtime performs the write. **Two boundaries this ruling does not cross, recorded so they are not read into it:** (1) **no backfill** — `DEC-20260731-05` §8 Q5, founder-ruled 2026-07-31; Phase 3 approval records created before activation are not in the ledger and never will be, so clause 2 is met *going forward*, not retroactively; (2) **"durable recording" is not "the Approval Queue is ledger-backed"** — that screen's `ledgerBacked` stays hardcoded `false` and its non-ledger disclosure still renders, correctly, because decisions *reach* the ledger while the queue *reads* from Supabase; wiring the queue remains a separate future decision. **The competing reading was put to the Founder and not adopted:** a strict reading of clause 2 would have held it open until the queue's disclaimer comes off; `builder` named it as not unreasonable, recommended against it on the grounds that clause 2 governs whether evidence is durable rather than which database a screen reads from, and the Founder ruled MET. **Evidence standing, stated rather than assumed:** `builder` flagged before the ruling that the supporting live figure — the delivery panel's `2 recorded` and two rows in `console_v1_console_approval_evidence` — was checked **2026-08-03T05:28:45.846Z** and was by then hours stale, and that Rule 7 and `DEC-20260801-02` bar 4 discourage resting a ruling on a re-quoted check; **no fresher check is recorded and none is claimed** — `builder` cannot run one, since `DEC-20260801-03` makes `app/server/ledger-read` the only authorized read path and its credential is deployment-side. The Founder ruled with that limitation on the record. **Propagation outstanding:** `founder-os-console` `CLAUDE.md` and the Approval Queue banner in `ApprovalQueue.tsx` still render clause 4's superseded phrase to the Founder on a live screen. *(Propagation update, same day: the Founder separately approved two workstreams — A `founder-os-console` (PR #40), B `FounderOS` (PR #203) — then, on a `builder` escalation that `DEC-20260725-01` clauses 13–14 and `design-architecture.md` §24 still presented Phase 3 as currently partial and durability as currently unmet, extended B to those locations. That extension is **marking only**: original text preserved unedited under dated `[SUPERSEDED 2026-08-03]` notes, and the **MVP milestone is expressly not moved**. The Founder's instruction, recorded as given: *"Do not move the MVP milestone, change the MVP gate, or imply that MVP status advanced because Phase 3 completed… The purpose of this edit is consistency of current-state guidance, not a change in governance."*)*

**MVP STATUS RULED UNCHANGED by the Founder 2026-08-03 — recorded as the Founder's ruling, not a `builder` inference.** Phase 3 completing does **not** by itself move MVP status. The governed position is three-part and must be reported as such: (1) **Phase 3 is complete**; (2) **MVP status is unchanged**; (3) **MVP completion continues to be governed independently** by `DEC-20260725-01` clauses 13–17 and the `design-architecture.md` §24 amendment **until separately ruled**, and that set has not been evaluated against the system's current state. **`DEC-20260801-07` (Verify Before Trusting) ruled active by the Founder
2026-08-01, and placed in the Constitution as Rule 7** — Founder direction
*"we need to create rules to verify before trusting"*, then *"put it in the
constitution"* on the placement question `builder` raised. Constitution
v1.10. Seven clauses: (1) live state needs a live check, named and dated,
never a re-quote; (2) executable content is executed before commit, against
stubs where real inputs are unsafe; (3) a claim about a record requires
reading the record — "unverifiable" requires having looked; (4) a doctrine
procedure is performed, not assumed, because partial compliance fails exactly
where full compliance was meant to catch; (5) review is not dispositioned
until it lands, **including re-review of a revised head**; (6) a document's
account of itself must match the document, which matters here because merge
commits inherit PR bodies (`DEC-20260721-01` item 4); (7) the same discipline
binds claims made **to** the system — a finding asserting live state is
checked live before being taken *or* declined. Clauses 1 and 5 generalise
`DEC-20260801-02` bars 4 and 2 from authorization-drafting to **every
governed artifact**, since every failure they were written from occurred
outside that scope. The constitution carries the operative text; the decision
carries the **dated instance behind each clause**, deliberately kept out of
`/01-constitution/` which states doctrine rather than case history. Adds
**no** merge gate, CI check, or automation, and creates no evidence class.
**Breach class is deliberately open** for Founder disposition — the decision
notes the instances are not all the same kind of act (a false assertion, a
skipped step that left no false claim, and a claim true when made that went
stale unattended) — and `builder` takes no position, since the rule governs
its own conduct. Prior same-day:
**`DEC-20260801-06` (Activation Sequencing for `DURABLE_PERSISTENCE_ENABLED`)
ruled active by the Founder 2026-08-01** — closes the open question
`HO-20260801-02` §4 raised and `DEC-20260725-01` clause 8 left implicit:
whether `DURABLE_PERSISTENCE_ENABLED` must be on when `LEDGER_BACKED` flips.
Ruled: **one Founder authorization covers both changes, executed
activate → verify → flip, and a failed verification spends the
authorization without flipping** — no re-use, a fresh authorization
required for any further attempt. The blocking verification, not a
calendar gap, is the control. Two options were declined on the record:
two separate authorizations on separate days, and reading condition 2 as
a point-in-time capability proof that permits flipping over a dark
writer — the latter declined **explicitly** so it cannot later be
recovered as a live reading by being unaddressed. Rationale: condition 2
was ruled met on a *time-boxed* controlled-writer window (durable row
`axn_a6590a9dcb564d38`) that was then closed, and `LEDGER_BACKED=true`
removes the Approval Queue's non-ledger disclosure — so flipping while
the writer is dark asserts durable backing for records nothing is
writing, the `agent-rules.md` §10 honesty class. **This moves no clause 8
condition** (standing unchanged: 2, 3, 4, 5 met; 1 open;
`LEDGER_BACKED=false`), authorizes no environment change, and does not
authorize the flip — it constrains how that authorization must be
executed when issued. Its §5 evidence requirement is live-check-with-
timestamp per `DEC-20260801-02` bar 4, and the decision deliberately
asserts no current value for the flag. Left open in §6: whether the flip
authorization must restate the ordering or may cite this decision by
identifier. **Revised to v1.1 2026-08-02** on three Founder rulings, taking
the substantive findings from a review round that landed after v1.0 had
already merged: the interval between verification and flip is bounded at
**30 minutes** (past it, re-verify in full); §5's freshness bound is capped
at **15 minutes**, since v1.0 let the operator choose the bound with no
ceiling and a thirty-day bound met its letter; and `DEC-20260729-01`
clause 6 is named a **blocking precondition** at §4 step 0, which v1.0 did
not cite at all. The merge-while-review-in-flight failure is R5 of
`DEC-20260801-07`'s Rule 7 catalogue, and this revision discharges it.

Constitution v1.12 marks the final box by transcription of the Founder's act. **`DEC-20260729-01` clause 6 item 8 — closed the same day, after the flip.** The Founder's PR #36 comment correctly stated the flip did not discharge it. The Founder then issued it separately, naming `f5d7549b25550a37911e2d9384757b4d8dd2e496`, the commit running in production throughout; clause 6 is now **eight of eight** (`DEC-20260729-01` v1.4). **The ordering is recorded, not smoothed:** `DEC-20260801-06` Step 0 makes clause 6 a blocking precondition to *beginning* the sequence, and the sequence ran while item 8 was open. The authorized SHA and the deployed SHA are the same, so the states agree — the order did not. `builder` raised the item before execution and did not re-raise it as blocking once underway; an independent review surfaced it post-flip. **`DEC-20260725-01` clause 8 condition 1 RULED MET by the Founder 2026-08-02 at 12:31 PM ET** — the first quarterly encrypted export ran that day (`founder-os-telegram` run `20260802T151426Z-fb844992`): four encrypted objects promoted into the governed Cloudflare R2 `evidence/` prefix, the archive restored into an isolated proven-empty target, stage 9 passed **153 of 153** checks, and both the primary and recovery custody identities independently decrypted the promoted archive with plaintext SHA-256 digests matching the manifest. The Founder also **authorized activation of `DURABLE_PERSISTENCE_ENABLED`**, satisfying the sixth Rule 3 checklist item, and the ruling is transcribed verbatim in that decision's implementation record (v1.4 at recording); all six `DURABLE_PERSISTENCE_ENABLED` boxes and clause 8 condition 1 are marked in `01-constitution/operational-discipline.md` (v1.11) by transcription of that act, not on `builder`'s authority. **Clause 8 standing: conditions 1, 2, 3, 4 and 5 ALL MET; `LEDGER_BACKED` remains `false`** — its own founder authorization is a separate act the ruling expressly did not give and which stays unmarked, so five of five conditions is not the predicate. **Two facts travel together and must not be collapsed:** the **export outcome was SUCCESS**, while the **orchestrator outcome was ABORTED at stage 11 with a non-zero exit** — it attempted the AWS S3 `get-object-retention` operation, which Cloudflare R2 does not implement. No record may claim the process exited zero, that all thirteen stages passed, that per-object AWS retention dates were observed, or that the automatic stage 13 record was emitted. Retention was verified through R2's provider-native prefix-scoped bucket lock (`evidence-seven-year-retention`, enabled, prefix `evidence/`, 2,562 days); that rule was read **after** promotion, so coverage at the promotion instant is inferred from Cloudflare's documented behaviour rather than observed, and because a bucket lock is account configuration an Admin-scoped credential can alter, the guarantee is **not** administrator-proof. The verifier defect is recorded as **Finding D**, with a fix **proposed** in `founder-os-telegram` PR #120 — open and unmerged at recording, so no landed fix is claimed; it is **not** a condition-1 gate and did not become one retroactively, but must be corrected before the next quarterly export. **Separately gated, and not closed by this ruling:** `DEC-20260729-01` clause 6 records its eighth item — *exact deployment SHA authorized* — as **open** and the durable-write gate as "still blocked", and states that clause 6 is a blocking precondition to `DEC-20260801-06`'s activation sequence; whether the 2026-08-02 ruling also discharges that item is the Founder's to say and is flagged, not decided, by `builder`. *(Repository-source claim read from `DEC-20260729-01` on `main`; no live control-plane check was run for this entry and none is asserted.)* Prior same-day: **`DEC-20260725-01` clause 8 condition 2 RULED MET by the Founder 2026-08-01** — the Founder executed the Track D controlled writer window personally under a SHA-named execution authorization (telegram PR #108 comment `5151799201`, naming `450a8311`), and ruled on the evidence pack merged as telegram PR #109 → `965d3408`: one production `action_outcomes` row written through the gated durable path by real runtime activity, `reconciliation_required` false, read back as `console_reader` on both correlated identifiers, and `evidence_writer` privileges verified — relation-level `INSERT` with no `SELECT`, and `has_column_privilege(..., 'SELECT')` false on all 14 columns (that check establishes the absence of a column-scoped SELECT grant; it is not a column-level INSERT check, which would be uninformative since a relation-level INSERT grant makes it true for every column). The ruling is quoted verbatim in that decision's implementation record (v1.3 at recording) with its comment URL; the pack's **seven deviations** — D1: the flag was already `true` ≈18 minutes before the authorization — were read by the Founder and did not change the ruling. **Clause 8 standing: conditions 2, 3, 4, 5 MET; condition 1 OPEN; `LEDGER_BACKED=false`** (this ruling moves no other condition, does not authorize permanent durable-persistence activation, and does not authorize the flip). a file created after the bundle's pin at `866f340`. `/plato` correctly
reported no grounding and named the reason ("added/renamed since this
snapshot was fetched"). Precisely: `DEC-20260720-04` item 6's fail-closed
gate **never fired** — the bundle was present, valid and injected, and
simply does not *contain* a file postdating its pinned `source_commit`.
That is a content-coverage gap, not a mechanism failure, and the honest
answer is emergent model behaviour rather than a control `-04` specifies
(corrected after Tier-1 flagged an earlier over-claim). **Executed
Tier-2 confirmation:** `switch_off` short-circuits before any filesystem
read, so a real mechanism failure yields a prompt with no snapshot to
reference — a provenance-dated decline is the opposite signature, and is
only producible when the bundle was present and injected. The result
therefore does **not** revive the withdrawn grounding-failure argument — it evidences **drift**: four
governance documents added since `866f340`
(`operational-discipline.md`, `DEC-20260724-02`, `DEC-20260724-01`,
`HO-20260724-01`), one of them binding constitution doctrine, are all
invisible to the bundle. It **amends**
`DEC-20260720-04` (static bundle demoted from primary grounding to
fallback; live read becomes primary) — founder ruling — rather than
superseding it, and incorporates `DEC-20260720-05` as the write-path
implementation reference. `builder` verification recorded in its Context:
(a) **closed by production evidence at v0.4** — the strategist context
bundle is present and current to FounderOS `main@866f340` and carries the
full WF-01–WF-17 registry; the pre-fix Railway deployment `eac88cd8`
logged `not injected (file_missing: ENOENT …)`, a line emitted only when
`STRATEGIST_CONTEXT_BUNDLE_ENABLED` is `'true'`, proving the gate was ON
and the sole cause was `npm run build` never copying `doctrine/` into
`dist/` (plus a 1024-token ceiling raised the same day). Fixed by
`founder-os-telegram` PR #57 — Tier-1 PASS, Tier-2 Opus 4.8
PASS-WITH-ADVISORIES, founder SHA-named authorization, merged
`main@04a6e824`, deployed `a79b16c9` with a clean startup. **The
grounding-failure argument for this decision is therefore withdrawn**,
leaving drift and founder-bottleneck as its only remaining grounds — a
correction that cuts against the decision and is recorded as such per
`agent-rules.md` §10; (b) the v0.2 read/write scope
named directories this repository does not have (`02-roles/`,
`03-models/`, `06-templates/`, `08-archive/`, root-level `system-index.md`
and `naming-conventions.md`), which taken literally would have excluded
`04-agents/` where the role and model registries actually live and
revoked a read grant already ratified under `DEC-20260720-05` item 3 —
the founder authored the corrected scope directly (items 1 and 3, all
paths verified); (c) `04-agents/` holds no agent session logs, so the
originating draft's stated reason for excluding it does not apply here.
Founder scope rulings (2026-07-24): live grounding extends to **all
runtime agents**; the constitution grant is **named files only**; `-04`
is **amended**; the acceptance test targets **post-`866f340`** content;
the decision proceeds on drift and founder-bottleneck grounds. Tier-2
remediation folded in: item 8 / threat T1 restated as defense-in-depth
(not "inert by construction"); item 10.9 given a fail-closed
prompt-composition assertion; threat T8 added. Ratification retires
`DEC-20260720-05` negative test 8; item 10.9 is its replacement, landing
CI-green in the same runtime PR. **v0.6** remediates a Tier-2
**REQUEST-CHANGES** (GPT-5.6 Sol, on-roster, static review;

`status: active` allowlist** — the reviewer's `blocked` counterexample
(`wf-06`) was confirmed; item 9
gained **per-surface fallback** (the `-04` bundle is strategist-only, so
every other surface's failure behavior was undefined); item 10.8's
"unreachable regardless of requested path" was **narrowed** to what path
allowlisting actually enforces, backed by a content scanner and a
disclosed residual risk; item 10.9's composition assertion is now
**instantiated per consuming surface**, with the reviewer's alternative
(narrow item 1 to a named surface list) recorded for the founder rather
than taken, since item 1's breadth is a founder ruling. **v0.7** fixed an
inconsistency the v0.6 remediation itself introduced, caught by a second
Tier-2 pass (Grok 4.5, off-roster under a founder per-review exception),
which otherwise returned the identical finding set: item 1 claimed the
bounding controls "apply identically to every consuming surface", which
the new per-surface fallback and per-surface verification made false.
Item 1 now separates what is uniform (path allowlist, status filter,
injection boundary, kill switch) from what is per-surface (fallback,

Tier-1 (and, independently, by a third Tier-2 pass on the same head): v0.6 had excluded

`source_of_truth: true` is a **ratified** state with operational
activation deferred — deferred activation is not deferred truth. Item 7
now admits, under `07-decisions/`, `source_of_truth: true` plus `active`
**or** `approved`; the mischaracterization is left on the record rather
than overwritten, since `builder` supplied it (see v0.10 for the

does not activate it repository-wide") and on

for execution" vs `active` = "currently guiding work"), rather than on

blaming reviewers for missing what they were never shown; Tier-1 caught

ratification.

`DEC-20260722-02` (proposed, 2026-07-22, `builder`/`claude-code`) is
**Read-Only Runtime Approval-Gate Exposure to Console**, the cross-repo
decision `DEC-20260721-02` clause 4 named as a prerequisite and declined
to authorize itself: a single new read-only endpoint,
`GET /console/approvals` on the founder-os-telegram Console API,
returning a fixed, closed-enum field allowlist restricted to active
holds only (`pending|claimed|executing`; no resolved/outcome exposure),
with a corresponding narrow amendment to `DEC-20260714-01` clause 7. It
authorizes no write path, no automation, no durable-store activation, and
no Console UI wiring — Phase 2 UI wiring stays separately gated per
`DEC-20260721-02` clause 4. Design-only; no code changed. **`v0.1`
received a GPT-5.6 Sol Tier-2 review (2026-07-22): REQUEST-CHANGES, 5
blocking findings** (untruncated clause-7 comparison; `action` bound to a
closed, code-verified enum and `approvalId` removed — proven to be the
literal Telegram callback mutation key, not inert; resolved/outcome
exposure removed entirely; the shared-credential justification replaced
with a genuinely separate, route-specific credential
`CONSOLE_APPROVALS_TOKEN` after finding no rotation practice documented
for the existing pair in either repo; an end-to-end no-store/no-cache/
no-log/no-trace/no-analytics clause added) — all addressed in `v0.2`; see
the decision's own Review Log for the full disposition.

`v0.2` then received its own Tier-2 review (2026-07-22): **REQUEST
CHANGES, 3 HIGH + 2 MEDIUM findings.** HIGH-1 (fail-closed) replaced
clause 1's per-record silent exclusion with whole-response validation
against all six fields by name and a `503`/`integrity-check-failed`
envelope on any failure — no partial lists. HIGH-2 (retention boundary)
expanded clause 5 to explicitly name and constrain runtime logs,
error-reporting SDKs, tracing/APM, any intermediary, hosting-platform
observability, and the future Console consumer's logs/analytics, with
mandatory header/token redaction and a fail-closed deployment rule.
MEDIUM-1 narrowed the rotation-practice claim to what an actual recorded
search (pattern `rotat`, both repos' working trees) can support. MEDIUM-2
added explicit credential lifecycle acceptance criteria (generation,
storage, rotation, revocation, fail-closed startup) rather than leaving
them silent. HIGH-3 (authority target) was explicitly **not** resolved
in `v0.3` — the review correctly identified that this decision amending
`DEC-20260714-01` clause 7 while that decision itself remains `proposed`
raised a real, founder-only governance question (does this amend a
non-binding proposal, retroactively ratify clause 7, or stand alone as
its own contract?); clause 2 presented all three without choosing.

`DEC-20260722-02` reached **`active` status on 2026-07-23.** HIGH-3
(authority target) was resolved in `v0.4` by founder choice **(c)
standalone contract** — this decision does not amend or depend on
`DEC-20260714-01`; its title changed accordingly (no longer framed as an
amendment). A same-vendor Opus 4.8 Tier-2 re-review of `v0.4`
(2026-07-23, disclosed substitute for the excluded GPT-5.6 cross-vendor
pass, per explicit founder authorization) independently verified the
document's security-critical code claims against `founder-os-telegram`
source and found 1 BLOCKING defect (clause 1's `createdAt`/`expiresAt`
specified as ISO strings when the store actually holds Unix epoch
milliseconds — would have permanently `503`'d), 1 HIGH defect (clause
1's "active holds" scope undefined against the real `approval:*`
keyspace, which also holds resolved records and a non-record claim-list
key), 1 MEDIUM (no startup check that `CONSOLE_APPROVALS_TOKEN` isn't
misconfigured equal to `CONSOLE_API_TOKEN`), and 2 ADVISORY findings (no
sample record JSON; clause 5's non-header requirements not mechanically
verifiable). The BLOCKING/HIGH findings were fixed in `v0.5`, the MEDIUM
in `v0.6`. **The founder ratified the decision 2026-07-23 (`v0.7`)**
with the 2 ADVISORY findings knowingly left open, deferred to whatever
implementation handoff eventually cites this decision — no such handoff
exists yet, so `/console/approvals` has zero code written against it as
of ratification. `status`/`source_of_truth` flipped together in the
activation commit, this repo's standard pattern.
